# DP Auth Wave 2 + External-Model MCP Exposure (refreshed 2026-09-12 to HEAD)

**Born 2026-08-22, with every anchor verified at the pre-parity commit `6c056870f`. Refreshed 2026-09-12 from an
18-agent audit — 9 readers and 9 adversarial skeptics — re-derived from the INDEX, against a tree that MOVED under
the audit: the readers pinned `3cc520afa`, the skeptics re-derived at `f4c55ee84`, `9621c0433` and `f32faaab6`, the
owner's dp-gate decision landed at `bcc1e79c3` during it, and this file's own spot-checks were taken at `96ce7b209`.
The filename keeps its birth date by repo convention (five tracked files cite it). EVERY COUNT IN THIS FILE IS A
READING WITH A DATE, not a standing fact — including the counts in this paragraph; re-run the probe rather than quote
the number.**

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or
> superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan row by row. Rows are tracked in the `## Tracker` table below,
> in the row grammar the repo's tooling reads (`| Row | Task | Status | Commit | Note |`); the Phase sections carry
> the full spec for each row. Update the row (glyph, commit sha, one-line note) IN THE SAME COMMIT that lands it,
> and run `python3 scripts/sort-plan-tracker.py docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-22-dp-auth-wave2-and-mcp-exposure.md`
> after any row addition.

**Goal:** (1) Close the auth gaps that survived 1.30.1 — the Pi card's unguarded stored-JWT fallback, the
single-flight swallowing `forceRefresh`, the model-fetcher's stale stored key — and make the silent 401 re-mint
failure diagnosable; support cli-only users (`NESSY_HOME`, managed-dp fallback). (2) Stop the silent exposure of
internal MCP tools (`mcp__dpJira__*`, `mcp__dpGitlab__*`, session tools) to external models, with an honest,
user-visible whitelist. (3) Port three MCP-pool hardening mechanisms measured in nessy-cli's F2 work.

**Sequencing:** the precondition is MET — `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-22-runtime-parity-closure.md` is CLOSED
(2026-09-11, release 1.36.0) — and a second plan, `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-11-coverage-95-parity.md`, has
since landed waves 1–19 over the same files and shipped 1.37.0. DA0.1 is therefore a mandatory re-anchor pass
against BOTH, and it is the only row that may dispatch first.

**Order in the queue (owner, 2026-09-12 ~22:35 — "хорошо, записывай"):** this plan executes AFTER the coverage-95 plan's
close-out — its last row C3.380 lifts the three runtimes (SDK 0.3.269, codex rust-v0.154.0, Pi 0.85.1 as read on
2026-09-12) and 1.38.0 ships — and BEFORE `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-10-integrations-and-authorization.md`. Reasons,
recorded: the user-facing auth failures this plan was born for outrank the parity tail; this plan is the smaller of the
two (26 open rows, mostly S/M, one L) and almost none of it depends on a runtime version, while the integrations plan's
I2 rows are written against the pre-lift versions and must be re-derived after the lifts anyway. DA0.1 (re-anchor) runs
once, after the lifts. Phase 1 (auth) MAY ship as its own release (1.39.0) so the fixes reach users before Phases 2–3
(external-model MCP exposure, pool hardening) land.

**Research base:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-22-dp-auth-user-401-and-nessy-cli-key-flow-research.md`
(5-agent research + adversarial verification; §6 row table, §7 dp-login follow-up) plus the external-model whitelist
research. **Read that spec by SYMBOL, never by its line numbers: its §7 C31 and C32 anchors into `pi-agent.ts` and
`unified-network-interceptor.ts` are all dead, while both findings verify TRUE at HEAD — a lane grepping it by line
will conclude the findings are refuted.** Two of its sentences are REFUTED rather than stale and must not seed
another row: §C's "our `ProcessSupervisorPort` covers the direct child. It does not cover `npx`/`uvx`/`pnpm dlx`
grandchildren" was already false when written (see DA3.1), and its §"Avoid / audit ourselves" item (b) attributes a
loopback-auth gap to "our copies" that is nessy-cli's and never existed here (see DA2.5). Key measured facts, as of
2026-09-12:

- nessy-cli's `EXTERNAL_MODEL_MCP_WHITELIST` (8 server names) filters per-request `tools[]`; classification is the
  `tgpt/` id prefix — and that IS the load-bearing signal there, which an earlier reading of ours inverted: their
  `dpModelsClient` reads the catalogue's `is_internal`, but `modelRegistry.resolveModelConfig` overwrites it
  unconditionally with `isInternalModel(config.id)` one hop later, so the prefix is what reaches
  `isMcpServerAllowedForModel`. Their gate has a boot-time fail-open, four unguarded accessors when this plan was
  written (closed at their HEAD except the `ensureTool` dispatch path) and a `tgpt/`-prefix bypass on BYO endpoints.
  Copy the concept, not the mechanism — and NOT their host matching, which is a `String.includes` that matches an
  attacker path segment on any host.
- Our exposure today, worded precisely: when dp servers are enabled — **28** builtin dp entries at HEAD, all
  `defaultEnabled: false`, plus 4 named exclusions (`packages/shared/src/mcp/builtin-dp-servers.ts`, symbols
  `BUILTIN_DP_MCP_SERVERS` and `BUILTIN_DP_MCP_EXCLUSIONS`; the plan's original "29" was a 2026-08-22 number carried
  forward as if it were a HEAD reading) — our full tool array reaches `llm-proxy.t-tech.team` unfiltered on all three
  runtimes. **The precondition understates the gap**: `mcp__session__*` is not pool-served and rides a DEFAULT
  configuration, so a pool-only filter cannot clear the measured refusal. dp-tool proof is the 403 echo transcribed
  in `docs/testing/cdp-runs/2026-07-30--2026-08-17-run-narratives.md`. The measured egress is to an internal
  corporate proxy: the one arm addressing a genuinely third-party model (`anthropic/*` via `/v1/messages`) is refused
  by the guardrail BEFORE forwarding, so no measured turn has put our tool array in front of a third-party model.
  The severity framing is "unpoliced egress to an internal proxy", not "leaked to Anthropic".
- `Nestor-Token` scoping to the verified gateway URL is CONFIRMED on all runtimes (adapter whole-URL match;
  interceptor prefix match — a deliberate, documented divergence, and DA1.10/DA1.11 are the two halves of narrowing
  it).
- **CORRECTED:** the plan used to say our adapter misdiagnoses a guardrail 403 as `invalid_api_key`, citing a branch
  of `packages/codex-responses-adapter/src/errors.ts` by line. That branch was UNREACHABLE in production and the
  live misdiagnosis was the transport-site status collapse; both halves are fixed and DA2.7 is closed.

## Target scheme (owner-approved, 2026-09-12 ~21:40)

**This is the end state every auth row in Phase 1 must land inside. It is an owner decision and binds the rows it
touches; where a row's 2026-08-22 text disagrees, this section wins and the row says so.**

1. **The gateway credential of record is ONLY the Nestor JWT in `~/.nessy/dp_auth_creds.json`.** A turn never waits
   on, and never touches, the dp CLI session on any runtime — the gate moved from the TURN to the TOOL (coverage row
   `C3.381`, landed `bcc1e79c3`). dp is needed in exactly two cases and no others: **re-minting the JWT** (a 401, or
   expiry) and **the dp MCP tools**, which carry their own sign-in state.
2. **A needed re-mint that meets needs-login NEVER blocks a turn.** Show the existing full-screen "Sign in to
   Developer Platform" card WITH THE REASON — dp not found / signed out / timed out / outdated / exchange failed,
   **codes only, never a token value or its length** — and ONE button that runs `dp auth login` for the user, as
   today. **No browser is opened without a click.** nessy-cli auto-logins; the owner has declined that for the
   desktop.
3. **The binary follows DP-1 as amended:** system dp first, our own managed copy only when the system one is absent
   or unrunnable. **The user's `~/.dp` session is SHARED and is never replaced by an isolated workdir** — nessy-cli's
   `~/.nessy/dp_latest` forces a second login, which is the failure this decision exists to avoid.
4. **dp exec budgets are 30 s**, for BOTH `dp version` and `dp auth print-token` (coverage row `C3.391`).
5. **Headless:** either align `NESSY_DP_AUTH_TOKEN` with nessy-cli's `NESSY_CLI_DP_AUTH_TOKEN` semantics, or
   DOCUMENT the difference — theirs is used straight as a Bearer, ours is exchanged through `/api/v2/token`.

**Recorded FALLBACK, a posture and not work:** if users still report authorization problems after this scheme lands,
switch to nessy-cli's model wholesale — a managed dp copy, our own dp session rather than the user's, and automatic
login. It is written down so the next owner sitting has a named alternative rather than a re-derivation; **no row in
this plan builds it, and none may start it without a new owner decision.**

**Which rows this scheme moves:** DA1.4 (the reason line is now REQUIRED, on the existing dp card, for all three
runtimes), DA1.5 (headless naming, clause 5), DA1.6 (clauses 3 and 4 pin the ladder's workdir and its budgets), and
DA1.12 (an expired JWT is the re-mint trigger clause 1 names, so it needs its own code). Each of those rows carries
a **Target scheme** paragraph naming the clause it answers.

## Refresh header — 2026-09-12

**This plan was written on 2026-08-22 and its anchors were verified at a PRE-PARITY commit. Everything below has
been re-derived from the INDEX at HEAD on 2026-09-12 by an 18-agent audit (9 readers + 9 adversarial skeptics);
where a skeptic corrected a reader, the skeptic's verdict is what this file carries.** Three things moved under it:

1. **The plan's sequencing precondition is MET.** `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-22-runtime-parity-closure.md`
   opens with `> **CLOSED — do not execute.** Closed 2026-09-11 with release 1.36.0 (P7.6)`. The parity plan did not
   merely close — it LANDED work this plan had rows for: the model-target classifier (P3.363), the managed-policy
   de-gate (P2.314 / P2.351 / P2.368), the MCP connection-isolation lever (P3.371) and both halves of the
   guardrail-403 misdiagnosis (P3.333 / P3.334). Those rows are re-stamped below with the shas.
2. **A SECOND plan has since reworked the same files.** `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-11-coverage-95-parity.md`
   has landed waves 1–19 and shipped 1.37.0. The re-anchor pass this plan's T0 orders is therefore twice the size it
   was written for, and its four named adjacencies are re-verified in DA0.1 rather than assumed.
3. **Line anchors are the disease this refresh cures.** Of the 20 line-numbered anchors this file used to carry,
   four still resolved, five had merely renumbered, and the rest were dead — one because the file changed PACKAGE,
   which renumbering cannot repair. **Every anchor in this file is now a `` `path` `` plus a symbol name.** Per
   AGENTS.md, a retired anchor is named in WORDS and never re-spelled in `path:line` form, because a correction that
   spells it re-creates the finding for every citation guard. Read this file by symbol; if a symbol has moved, that
   is a finding to record, not a reason to distrust the claim beside it.

**What the audit changed, in one paragraph.** One row is LANDED and closes (W7). Five are PARTIALLY landed by other
plans and must not be re-executed as written (W1, W2, W6, H2, H3). One is REFUTED as a defect and collapses to two
documentation sentences (W5). One had its status word corrected from *partially landed* to *open* because the
evidence credited to it predates the row by fourteen days (T9). The rest are open, with their premises corrected in
place. Seven rows are NEW, from a read of the nessy-cli delta since 2026-08-22. Nothing in this plan has ever been
executed: the file has exactly two commits (`6dff9f450`, which committed it, and `c5a1f8bcd`, which corrected the W7
note), and every row carried the pre-grammar `⏳` glyph with an empty commit cell until this refresh.

## Owner decisions ledger (2026-08-22, chat)

**These are the owner's decisions and are reproduced unchanged. Dated reading notes are appended BENEATH each where
HEAD has moved a premise the decision rests on; no note overrides a decision.** The only conversion applied to the
recorded text is that DP-1's two parenthetical EVIDENCE annotations, which were line anchors and are both dead today
(one of them because the file moved package), are re-spelled as symbol citations — every word of the decision itself
is untouched, and the dead anchors are named in words in the reading note.

- **DP-1 (amended 2026-08-23, owner's managed-dp request — confirm wording at review):** the fallback becomes a
  *managed copy we own*. Measured cause: a corp-installed `/usr/local/bin/dp` (root:wheel, dir `drwxr-xr-x`, not
  user-writable) can never `rename()` itself during `dp update` — a stale system dp is PERMANENTLY stale for a
  non-root user. We therefore (i) keep the system dp first; (ii) add our own copy under `<CONFIG_DIR>/dp/`
  (`~/.nessy-agent`); (iii) reuse nessy-cli's copy read-only when present; (iv) download a **pinned** version only
  when nothing usable exists. "Never download" is withdrawn; "never bundle" and "never write another product's
  directory" stand. Needs-login on a runnable system dp still does NOT descend the ladder; *absent, unrunnable, or
  below-floor* does. The 2026-08-22 wording (`DP_WORKDIR=~/.nessy/dp_latest`) is REVOKED — that workdir lies inside
  `~/.nessy`, which our sandbox floor lists as a denied read root (`packages/session-tools-core/src/runtime/credential-deny-roots.ts`
  symbol `credentialDenyRoots`), so the write grant would be refused
  (`packages/server-core/src/bootstrap/host-sandbox-settings.ts` symbol `withDpStateRootWriteGrant`); measured, see DA1.6.

  > **Reading note, 2026-09-12 — the decision stands, and its SANDBOX basis is now conditional while a stronger,
  > sandbox-independent one has appeared.** (a) Both of DP-1's evidence citations were line anchors into
  > `credential-deny-roots.ts` and `host-sandbox-settings.ts`; the first is dead beyond repair because the file moved
  > from the shared package to `packages/session-tools-core/src/runtime/` (the tree carries
  > `packages/shared/src/agent/__tests__/relocated-credential-deny-roots.test.ts` marking the move), and the second had
  > only renumbered. Both are cited by symbol above. (b) `packages/shared/src/config/sandbox-settings.ts` symbol
  > `SANDBOX_ENABLED_DEFAULT` is `false` by standing owner decision (2026-08-12/13), so `withDpStateRootWriteGrant`'s
  > refusal binds only where an operator turns the sandbox on. **The revocation of `~/.nessy/dp_latest` survives on a
  > basis that did not exist when DP-1 was written:** `~/.nessy/**` is now in the default `deniedPaths`
  > (`packages/shared/src/agent/mode-types.ts`, added by P6.674 `e6296c0f7`), which is the PERMISSION-layer floor and
  > bites with isolation off. Rewrite the sandbox sentences in DA1.6 accordingly; do not delete the conclusion.
  > (c) Both rungs DP-1 names survive both floors at HEAD: `credentialDenyRoots` contributes only
  > `<configDir>/credentials.enc` from the config dir, and `deniedPaths` carries no `~/.nessy-agent/**`.
  > (d) **Rung 1 of the ladder should be re-named before it is built.** The ladder's `NESSY_DP_PATH` occurs in ZERO
  > files of nessy-cli. The surface's owner has published a name and a resolver for exactly this — `NESSY_DP`, a
  > `${NESSY_DP}` placeholder resolvable in an MCP server's `command`, defaulting to `getDpFilePath()` with a
  > user-set `process.env.NESSY_DP` winning (nessy-cli `a7b7e2374`, AIDT-7054, with its own fork-patch note
  > `docs/fork-patches/mcp-managed-dp-placeholder.md`). Per §READ-do-not-INVENT, take the owner's name or write down
  > why we decline it.

- **DP-2:** Scope: MCP-hardening rows №7 (descendant-pid sweep), №8 (HTTP/SSE share gate), №9 (pool-key credential
  test) are IN this plan (Phase 3), plus №6 (loopback MCP server auth audit) in Phase 2.

  > **Reading note, 2026-09-12.** Three of the four have moved without this plan touching them: №8 and half of №9
  > landed as parity P3.371 (`09d3e94af`), and №6 is REFUTED — the defect was never in this tree. №7 survives, but
  > re-scoped: the path the row names already group-kills, and the leak is on a branch the row never names. See
  > DA3.1–DA3.3 and DA2.5.

- **DP-3:** Win/Linux publish: an in-plan "verify + prepare" row; launching publish jobs stays user-owned.

  > **Reading note, 2026-09-12.** Unchanged as a decision, and the row is cheaper than it looks: the evidence it asks
  > for has been sitting in `.gitlab-ci.yml` since before the row was written. See DA1.9.

- **DP-4:** Whitelist classification is host-side and per-model (connection host + model vendor), NOT a model-id
  prefix; one predicate module, enforcement identical across all three runtimes (parity principle — three
  per-runtime implementations are how parity erodes).

  > **Reading note, 2026-09-12 — honoured, and already BUILT as a predicate.** `classifyModelTarget`
  > (`packages/shared/src/agent/backend/internal/drivers/upstream-target.ts`) landed with parity P3.363 in the exact
  > module DP-4 asks for, beside `isNessyGatewayBaseUrl`, host-side and shared by all three runtimes. Its verdict is
  > ADVISORY — the module says "Nothing in this tree withholds a tool on it" — because the owner deferred the
  > withholding half (see the note under DP-5). Two of DP-4's clauses are refined by what landed: the verdict is six
  > classes, not two, and the host is PARSED, never substring-matched.

- **DP-5 (2026-08-22, chat):** The whitelist restricts ONLY the corporate proxy channel (llm-proxy-hosted
  external-vendor models). BYO endpoints (api.anthropic.com, api.openai.com, any user-added host) and the future
  subscription cards (Claude Pro/Max, ChatGPT) are NOT restricted — "когда откроем подписки, у тех кто на подписке и
  не через llm-proxy должно всё работать". Deliberate consequence, recorded consciously: a BYO connection straight to
  a third-party vendor gets the full tool set (incl. dp MCP servers if the user enabled them — they are default-off);
  that is the user's own credential and choice, not the corp channel.

  > **Reading note, 2026-09-12 — DP-5 now COLLIDES with a shipped default, and the collision is why the
  > enforcement half has no code.** `apps/electron/src/renderer/components/apisetup/byo-runtime.ts` symbol
  > `DEFAULT_ENDPOINT_URL` is `'https://llm-proxy.t-tech.team'`: the host DP-5 calls "the corporate proxy channel" is
  > the BYO card's DEFAULT endpoint, so a host list with that entry restricts every BYO user — the population DP-5
  > exempts by name. That is why the landed predicate carries no corp-proxy host list at all and classifies on
  > endpoint KIND plus model NAMESPACE, and why the landed test table deliberately calls a BYO endpoint AT the vendor
  > `external-vendor` / `restricted: true` — a DISCLOSURE is right under either resolution of the collision, a
  > WITHHOLDING is not. **The owner has already ruled on the consequence, on 2026-09-05 (parity sitting §1.3, on row
  > P3.363, option B): "leave the model-target tool filter to its own plan after this one closes — the disclosure
  > notice already made today's state honest, and the build half still carries an unsettled design collision plus a
  > live-network probe series."** The parity plan closed; no successor plan exists under `docs/superpowers/plans/`;
  > the parity sitting text names the successor as "its own plan … with the mandatory re-anchor pass its rows already
  > name", and this is the only plan whose rows name such a pass. **THIS PLAN IS THAT PLAN**, and DA2.1 must settle
  > the DP-5 collision before DA2.2 or DA2.3 can dispatch.

  > **Axis note, 2026-09-17 (DA1.71, after DA2.1 and DA2.14 landed):** this clause governs TWO questions and answers one. The
  > DISCLOSURE half closed NAMESPACE-ONLY under DA2.1 (`6d059a686`), and the WITHHOLDING half was unblocked by the owner on
  > 2026-09-17 (DA2.14 option (a), built as DA2.2 half A) on ALL connection kinds. So connection KIND — corporate proxy vs BYO vs
  > subscription — is REFUSED as an axis for both; the axis is the model NAMESPACE (plus the `unclassified` posture DA2.2 states).
  > The exemption this clause writes for the subscription cards is therefore implemented nowhere and is not owed: a subscription
  > card whose model id carries a vendor namespace is disclosed and withheld like any other connection, and one whose id is
  > unprefixed lands in `unclassified`, which withholds too (WI-e measured both cards, A3.14 item 4; the disclosure asymmetry
  > between the two cards is D1.191 of the integrations plan). The Russian sentence above stands as the owner's intent for the
  > USER experience — a subscription must "just work" — which the model-side rule satisfies wherever the id is namespaced.

## Resolved decision rows (2026-08-22, chat — nothing left to discuss)

**Reproduced unchanged; reading notes appended beneath.**

- **ODR-1 → measurement-gated branch (owner: "мы только потребители" — no proxy-owner negotiation exists as an
  option).** W4 starts with a 10-minute probe series against llm-proxy `/v1/messages` on an `anthropic/*` model:
  (i) zero tools; (ii) native-shape tools only (no `mcp__*`); (iii) whitelisted `mcp__*` subset only. The result
  selects the pre-committed branch — no discussion needed:
  - **A** — native tools pass, `mcp__*` blocked → W4 ships "strip all `mcp__*` on that wire" (the whitelist is
    effectively empty for anthropic-on-corp-proxy) + the W6 session notice names it.
  - **B** — any tool-carrying request is blocked → W4 ships chat-only mode for anthropic-on-corp-proxy models (tools
    omitted entirely, honest UI notice "инструменты недоступны на этой модели"), turns work instead of 403ing.
  - **C** — the whitelisted subset passes → the regular W2/W3 whitelist applies unchanged.

  > **Reading note, 2026-09-12 — the series is now TWO probes, not three, and branch C is off the table.**
  > (a) **Arm (i) is already MEASURED on the rig**, not merely answered from a memory: `docs/testing/cdp-runs/2026-08-21-t23-lane-g.md`
  > records "the tool-free probe in the same run answered `ok wire=anthropic-messages`" on the entitled
  > `claude-custom-2` credential. (b) **Arm (iii) is NOT answered** — the recorded 403 was produced by a turn carrying
  > the WHOLE tool array, and a body enumerating every `mcp__` tool in an all-tools request says nothing about a
  > whitelisted-SUBSET request, which is the entire content of arm (iii). An earlier reading called it "answered
  > negative"; that reading converted an unrun probe into a settled verdict and is withdrawn here so no lane skips
  > it. A second correction to the same evidence: the raw body transcribed in that narrative names
  > `mcp__plugin_figma_figma__*` and `mcp__session__*` and is self-described as truncated; the `mcp__dpGitlab__*`
  > name often quoted for it comes from a SUMMARY, and it is the one name in that list that could bear on arm (iii)
  > at all, because `dpGitlab` is one of nessy-cli's eight whitelisted servers. (c) **Branch C defers to W2/W3, which
  > the owner sent to its own plan on 2026-09-05** — so C is not selectable as written; if the subset passes, the
  > outcome is "no wire change beyond what DA2.2/DA2.3 build", stated as such. (d) Where a branch may land is decided
  > by a page, not by convenience: `llm-gateway-protocol.md#feature-pass-through` says to inspect without modifying

  > branch A written at the wire contradicts both on the PASSTHROUGH arm — which is the arm this probe is about.

- **ODR-2 → executor-drafted.** UI copy for the badge/notice is drafted by the executor within the W6 spec's
  semantics (DP-5 vocabulary); owner reviews wording once, at C1 close-out. Not blocking.

  > **Reading note, 2026-09-12.** Still executor-drafted, and the copy no longer needs to be invented: nessy-cli

## Global Constraints

**Refreshed 2026-09-12. Everything not marked CORRECTED is unchanged from 2026-08-22.**

- Bun for everything; **branch `feat/TCODE-000` (CORRECTED — the plan said `bugfix/TCODE-000`; that branch exists
  locally and on the remote, but every commit since has landed on `feat/TCODE-000`, so the constraint as written
  would strand a lane on a branch nobody else is on)**; ONE commit per row, message given in the row; NO
  `Co-Authored-By` trailers; no MRs/tags.
- **CORRECTED — commit in the ATOMIC form and never with a bare `git add`.** `git commit -F <msgfile> -- <pathspec>`
  stages and commits exactly the named files in one step; a bare `git add` in a shared tree commits another lane's
  staged index, and `--amend` has already rewritten a sibling lane's message once. Zone parallel work by FILE.
- Token values NEVER appear in logs, errors, or test names — lengths/booleans only.
- `~/.nessy/dp_auth_creds.json` writer stays `packages/shared/src/auth/dp/` only; `oauth_creds.json` stays
  read-only. **(Reading note: at nessy-cli HEAD this is no longer a shared-writer file at all — see DA1.8's note and
  the AGENTS.md hand-back in this plan's §"What this refresh did not verify".)**
- `packages/codex-responses-adapter` must NOT import from `@nessy-agent/shared` (cycle). This is why
  `defaultCredsPath` and `isJwtShaped` are hand-duplicated in `packages/codex-responses-adapter/src/nestor-auth.ts`
  under their own cycle-avoidance docblock, and it is a constraint on DA1.12's fix shape.
- i18n: every new key in BOTH `en.json` and `ru.json`, alphabetical; gate
  `bun run lint:i18n:parity && bun run lint:i18n:sorted`.
- `mock.module`: spread AND restore, and restore from the SNAPSHOT, never from the live namespace (repo trap;
  `bun scripts/check-mock-module-spread.ts` is the instrument).
- Verification budget: per-row focused tests + affected-package typecheck; full regression once at close-out.
- Do not modify `.references/`, CI (beyond DA1.9's read-only verification), or fastlane.
- **CORRECTED — the close-out version is NOT pinned here and is NOT "the next bump".** The plan's original
  instruction ("parity's own close-out lands first") is SPENT: parity closed at 1.36.0 and the coverage plan has

## Ownership — who executes the auth rows (OWNER QUESTION, open)

**The overlap is real, it is by design, and it is stated in both files.** `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-10-integrations-and-authorization.md`
carries this plan's ENTIRE auth phase minus one row, as its Phase A3, with HEAD readings taken on 2026-09-10:

| This plan | Integrations plan | This plan | Integrations plan |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| DA0.1 (T0 re-anchor) | `A3.15` ("Sequence FIRST in this phase") | DA1.6 (T6 managed dp) | `A3.13` |
| DA1.1 (T1 forceRefresh) | `A3.6` | DA1.10 (T10 Pi prefix) | `A3.16` |
| DA1.2 (T2 Pi fallback) | `A3.9` (with T13) | DA1.11 (T11 gateway-host) | `A3.11` |
| DA1.3 (T3 model fetch) | `A3.7` | DA1.12 (T12 adapter expiry) | `A3.8` |
| DA1.4 (T4 diagnosable) | `A3.12` | DA1.13 (T13 snapshot) | `A3.9` (with T2) |
| DA1.5 (T5 `NESSY_HOME`) | `A3.10` | DA1.7 + DA1.8 (T7, T8) | `A3.17` |

**Two facts a decision needs.** (1) **DA1.9 (T9) is carried NOWHERE else** — the integrations plan declines it by
name as release infrastructure the owner runs. (2) **DA2.1–DA2.7 (W1–W7) and DA3.1–DA3.5 (H rows) are carried
nowhere else either**: that plan's §"Do not duplicate what already exists" maps only the T-rows and never mentions a
W-row, and the coverage plan scores zero for `classifyModelTarget`, `upstream-target`, `P3.363` and `onOutboundTools`.
So the MCP-exposure phase the owner sent to "its own plan" is visible in this file and in no other.

**The integrations plan states the decision as a one-liner in its own header:** *"If the owner would rather execute
the wave-2 plan directly, `A3.6`–`A3.13`, `A3.16` and `A3.17` close as moved and this phase keeps only `A3.1`–`A3.5`,
`A3.14` and `A3.15`."*

- **(A) — RECOMMENDED. Execute the auth rows FROM THIS PLAN.** It holds the owner decisions DP-1…DP-5 and ODR-1/ODR-2,
  which exist nowhere else, and its specs are fuller (the integrations rows are deliberately one-sentence verdicts
  plus a pointer, and three of them state their subject imprecisely — `A3.17` inverts the DA1.8 defect and names a
  shipping path that does not exist, `A3.10` overstates the `NESSY_HOME` absence, and `A3.9`'s wording is easy to
  misread as refuting DA1.2 when it refutes only the live-creds half). On a yes, `A3.6`–`A3.13`, `A3.16` and `A3.17`
  close as MOVED with a pointer to the DA row, in the integrations plan's own close-out commit, by whoever owns that
  file — **not by this plan and not by a lane executing it.**
- **(B) — the reverse.** The integrations plan keeps the auth rows; this plan drops DA1.1–DA1.8 and DA1.10–DA1.13 to
  pointers and keeps only DA0.1, DA1.9, the Phase 2 W-rows, the Phase 3 H-rows and the new nessy-cli delta rows. The
  cost is that the owner decisions and the fuller specs then live in a file nobody executes from.

**Until the owner answers, the default is (A) and NOTHING IS MOVED.** Both files keep their rows; the first lane to
dispatch a row on either side must check the twin's glyph before starting (all seventeen `A3.*` rows read `⬜` with
an empty commit cell at HEAD, so nothing has moved upstream and no scope has shrunk). **A DA row and its A3 twin must
never run in parallel** — they are the same files, which is the shared-file collision AGENTS.md forbids. Two twins
are file-zoned against DA2.1 as well: `A3.5` and `A3.16` both cite
`packages/shared/src/agent/backend/internal/drivers/upstream-target.ts`, DA2.1's module.

| DA3.1 | H1 — stdio teardown leaves grandchildren behind on the UNSUPERVISED path (№7, re-scoped) | ✅ | `751cef5f5` | `[host]` `[pi]` `[claude]` `[codex]` `[test]` Size M (was S). **The row's premise is REFUTED where it points and the defect survives where it does not.** The path it names already kills the GROUP: every supervised spawn sets `detached` off win32 and `signalTree` issues a negative-pid kill on POSIX and a `/T` taskkill on Windows, proven by a real grandchild-marker test — and BOTH mechanisms predate this plan (`feba3c424` 2026-07-22, `b0053399e` 2026-07-19), so the research base's sentence was wrong when written, not stale. What LEAKS is the branch the row never names: a stdio MCP config WITHOUT `pluginRuntime` takes the vendored SDK's `StdioClientTransport`, which spawns with no `detached` and closes by signalling the DIRECT CHILD — and `NessyMcpClient.close()` adds nothing above it. That branch serves every native, project and user `.mcp.json` stdio server, all 28 builtin dp servers, and `packages/shared/src/mcp/validation.ts` symbol `validateStdioMcpConnection`, a third unsupervised spawn site whose own comment names `uv tool run`, `npx` and `pipx`. SCOPE CORRECTION: the dp set is NOT npx-shaped — `materializeBuiltinDpMcpServers` emits the RESOLVED absolute dp path — so for it the residue is dp's own children, a different and unmeasured claim; the wrapper-shaped populations are the user-written entries and the validator. NATIVE-FIRST: the host already has TWO mechanisms — the supervisor's group kill and `packages/shared/src/mcp/headers-helper.ts`, which runs its helper in its own process group for exactly this reason — so the cheap honest fix is to give the unsupervised branch the same detached-group treatment, NOT to port nessy-cli's process-table sweep, which exists because their transports are not detached. Keep the sweep only as the named residue for a descendant that leaves the group. Validate on a PACKAGED build. Coverage `C3.103` is adjacent but drives BASH children, which are group-killed, so it will come back green while this leak is untouched — add the MCP arm deliberately. **WB-e 2026-09-17 — BUILT (`751cef5f5`).** NATIVE-FIRST answered NO, measured: vendored `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk` 1.29.0's `StdioClientTransport` has nothing for process groups (`detached` → 0 occurrences over `dist/esm`, control `spawn` in `client/stdio.js` → 6) and a group kill needs the child to be a group LEADER, so it cannot be added from outside. New `packages/shared/src/mcp/managed-stdio-transport.ts` symbol `createManagedStdioTransport`, used by BOTH spawn sites (`client.ts`'s non-plugin stdio branch and `validation.ts` symbol `validateStdioMcpConnection`; the SDK's own transport is the negative control), exposing spawn parameters as `params`. POSIX: spawn `detached`, signal the GROUP. **The note's native-first clause was INCOMPLETE:** the detach alone does not close the leak — the SDK's close ladder waits on the child's `close` event (the stdio PIPES) and a forked descendant inherits stdout, so the grace is spent waiting for the leaked process; the fix is `exit` + an UNCONDITIONAL group SIGTERM (`close` at ~1.2 s vs `exit` at ~15 ms, real-process probe). Windows arm DELEGATES to the SDK transport (keeps `cross-spawn`'s `.cmd` shims) and is graceful-only: sweeping descendants there needs `taskkill /T /F` while the leader is ALIVE, removing every Windows MCP server's clean shutdown → owner decision DA3.7. **Review R14 → row DA3.13, born and closed by RF-c (`c1bf9a971`):** both fix arms asserted an absence defended only by a control on the OTHER transport; a same-transport positive control now prices each arm's own window. |

## Phase 0 — Re-anchor

### DA0.1 (T0): Re-anchor pass — evidence only, no code

**Files:** this plan; no source file changes.
**Behavior:** verify every anchor in this plan against HEAD, re-derived from the INDEX (`git show HEAD:<path>`) and
never from the working tree, then update this file in one commit. **Sequence FIRST; no other row may dispatch before
it.** The precondition is met — the parity plan is CLOSED — but the drift has DOUBLED, because the coverage-95 plan
has since landed waves 1–19 over the same files and shipped 1.37.0.

## Phase 1 — Auth

### DA1.1 (T1): `forceRefresh` must not join a warm in-flight ensure

**Files:** `packages/shared/src/auth/dp/index.ts` (symbol `ensureNessyGatewayAuth`, the single-flight at
`if (inflight) return inflight`, which runs before `runEnsure` ever sees `forceRefresh`); tests in
`packages/shared/src/auth/dp/__tests__/index.test.ts`. **Compose with the SECOND single-flight layer the original
row did not name:** `apps/electron/src/main/handlers/nessy-auth.ts` symbol `createGatewayAuthController` keeps its own
`inflight` around `ensure()`, so a fix that changes only the shared module would SEEM to leave the Sign-in click able to join a stale forced run — CORRECTED by WA-a 2026-09-13: the second layer needed NO code. `createGatewayAuthController` only ever ORIGINATES forced runs; the one surviving join is into that run's own FALLBACK, which is non-forced by design, and splitting there would re-derive the same failure and could spend the user's second browser prompt.
**Behavior:** a `forceRefresh: true` caller that arrives while a non-forced ensure is running must NOT receive that
run's (possibly just-rejected) result. Await the in-flight run (result discarded, errors swallowed), then run a forced
ensure; concurrent forced callers share ONE forced run. Non-forced callers keep today's join semantics. Replace the
`forceRefresh` docblock's acceptance of the swallow with the measured reason it is not acceptable: `attemptAuthRetry`
gates on `managed.authRetryAttempted`, sets it BEFORE dispatch, and resets it only on a new message — so the retry
fires exactly once per message and there is no second firing to close the loop.
**Two production forced callers** can meet the warm launch ensure: `SessionManager` symbol
`refreshGatewayAuthForRetry`, and `nessy-auth.ts` symbol `runForcedThenFallback` (the Sign-in click).
**Tests:** (pos) a forced caller arriving during a warm in-flight gets a re-minted JWT and the dp exchange is invoked;
(neg) two concurrent forced callers produce ONE dp exchange; non-forced join unchanged. The existing forced-path cases
cover only the no-in-flight arm and stay green either way — the join case has no test at all. **An existing pin
constrains the fix shape:** the case asserting that two parallel NON-forced calls run `dp auth login` once encodes
today's join semantics and must stay green, which is why the fix is "await, then re-run forced" and never "drop the
single-flight".
**Native mechanism:** none on any runtime — `dp` is corporate tooling with no Claude, codex or Pi analogue, so this is
host tier 3. The reference has no such bug because it has no warm path to join: their content generator re-authenticates
unconditionally on any auth error, and their single-flight wraps only the dp-token half.
**Commit:** `fix(auth): forceRefresh bypasses the in-flight warm ensure`

### DA1.2 (T2): the Pi gateway card's stored-credential fallback fails CLOSED

**Files:** `packages/shared/src/agent/pi-agent.ts` (symbol `getPiAuth`: `getNestorGatewayJwt()` first, then the
unguarded `credentialManager.getLlmApiKey(slug)`); tests.
**Behavior:** on a GATEWAY-classified connection (`pi_compat` plus `isNestorCliBaseUrl`) the `getLlmApiKey` fallback
value must pass `isJwtShaped`; a non-JWT snapshot reads as "no credential" (fail closed, to the honest needs-login
path) and never leaves as `Nestor-Token`. BYO `pi_compat` connections are untouched — an arbitrary key is legitimate
there.
**Premise narrowed, and the narrowing does not kill the row.** The LIVE path IS guarded: symbol
`resolveNestorGatewayJwtForPi` reads through `packages/shared/src/auth/dp/creds-store.ts` symbol `getValidCreds`,
which checks `expiresAt` AND `isJwtShaped` (the 1.30.1 fix). So this row is the FALLBACK beneath it and nothing else.
What carries the value out is `packages/shared/src/unified-network-interceptor.ts` symbol `rewriteNestorAuthHeaders`,
which turns any Bearer on a URL under the CLI prefix into `Nestor-Token`.
**State the residue rather than imply it:** `packages/shared/src/auth/dp/jwt.ts` symbol `isJwtShaped` is a
three-non-empty-dot-segment structural test with no decode and no claim check, so a stale — or merely well-formed —
snapshot still passes and still goes out. That half is DA1.13, and this row must not be read as closing it.
**Tests:** (pos) garbage stored key plus a gateway baseUrl gives no auth sent (the 'surfaced needs-login' half is DA1.4/DA1.13's SURFACE, not this module's — WA-d 2026-09-13: `pi-agent.ts` carries no needs-login surface at HEAD, and the refusal reaches the wire as 'no credential' because the one-click card submits a `customEndpoint`, so the caller's `(!piAuth && !isCustomEndpointMode)` guard skips the legacy key path); (neg) a BYO
endpoint with a non-JWT key sends the key unchanged; and one that NAMES the residue — a well-shaped stale snapshot
still passes, with the DA1.13 pointer in the assertion's comment.
**Native mechanism:** none, and the honest note is that we are already stricter than the product we forked the flow
from: their token manager checks expiry only, with no shape test and no per-connection stored key to fall back to.
**Commit:** `fix(auth): the Pi gateway card refuses a non-JWT stored credential`

### DA1.3 (T3): gateway model refresh authenticates with live creds, never an empty token

**Files:** `packages/server-core/src/model-fetchers/nestor.ts` (symbol `fetchNestorModels`, the
`'Nestor-Token': credentials.apiKey ?? ''` header) and `packages/server-core/src/model-fetchers/index.ts` (symbol
`ModelRefreshService._doRefresh`, which calls the injected credential resolver). **Put the fix in the fetcher or the
service, NOT in a resolver: there are TWO production injections of `initModelRefreshService`** — one in
`apps/electron/src/main/index.ts` and one in `packages/server/src/index.ts`, the headless `nessy-server` arm — and both
resolve `manager.getLlmApiKey(slug)`. A fix written to the electron site alone ships the headless arm unfixed.
**Behavior:** authenticate from `getValidCreds()` (`@nessy-agent/shared/auth/dp`) instead of the stored connection
key; with no valid creds, skip the fetch under the existing warn-only contract and NEVER issue a request carrying an
empty or non-JWT `Nestor-Token` — an empty one reproduces the gateway's "invalid number of segments" 401, and a stale
one 401s while chat is healthy, which the user reads as *the model list is broken*.
**Two things the original row did not say.** (1) The fetcher already classifies its failures precisely (symbol
`classifyNestorModelsStatus`, with the unparseable-credential diagnosis from P3.90) — a credential REFUSED before the
request is issued must not lose that diagnosis, so the skip needs its own log line rather than reusing the fetch-failed
one. (2) Scope it by the predicate the fetcher already uses (symbol `isNestorConnection`, a prefix match) and say in
the comment that this is the PREFIX matcher and not `isNessyGatewayBaseUrl`'s whole-URL one; `model-fetchers/index.ts`
already warns about that difference, and DA1.10 owns the prefix half.
**Reach, measured:** the nestor branch is gated by `isCompatProvider` (`packages/shared/src/config/llm-connections.ts`),
which is `providerType === 'pi_compat'` — so this failure mode is reachable on ALL THREE gateway cards — CORRECTED by WA-c 2026-09-13: `packages/server-core/src/model-fetchers/registry.ts` symbol `GatewayRuntimeModelFetcher` is registered for `codex-runtime` and `claude-runtime` and reaches the same fetcher (this said 'ONLY on the Pi gateway card'),
which is the same connection DA1.2 reads from the other side.
**Tests:** (pos) a valid creds file puts the JWT in the header; (neg) absent or garbage creds issue no request and log
one warn.
**Native mechanism:** none — the gateway catalogue is supplied host-side on all three runtimes. But this is the one row
with a direct reference implementation to copy: their models client reads valid credentials, warns "No valid DP
credentials for loading models" and returns an empty list — exactly the behaviour specified here.
**Commit:** `fix(models): gateway model refresh authenticates with live dp creds`

### DA1.4 (T4): the silent re-mint failure becomes diagnosable, on all three cards

**Target scheme clause 2 governs this row.** A needed re-mint that meets needs-login never blocks a turn; the user
sees the existing full-screen "Sign in to Developer Platform" card WITH THE REASON and one button that runs
`dp auth login`. No browser opens without a click. Codes only, never a token value or its length.

**Files:** `packages/server-core/src/sessions/SessionManager.ts` (symbol `refreshGatewayAuthForRetry`, whose catch
discards the `DpAuthServiceError` under the comment "best-effort — the retry itself reports the real failure"; and
symbol `attemptAuthRetry`, the SHARED seam every runtime's auth failure passes through);
`apps/electron/src/renderer/components/chat/AuthRequestCard.tsx` (which already renders
`t('chat.dpAuth.errors.<code>')` off a `dpErrorCode`); i18n en+ru ONLY if a new code is minted.
**Behavior:** capture the `DpAuthServiceError.code` from the failed forced re-mint, carry it on the managed session,
and render it as the reason on the card the user is looking at.

**Three corrections the row must absorb.**
1. **The vocabulary and its localisation ALREADY EXIST.** `packages/shared/src/auth/dp/errors.ts` symbol `DpAuthError`
   has eight members (`DpNotFound`, `NeedsLogin`, `LoginFailed`, `ExecFailed`, `ExchangeFailed`, `Timeout`,
   `Cancelled`, `InteractiveLoginUnavailable`) and both locale files carry all eight as `chat.dpAuth.errors.<Code>`.
   So this row is CAPTURE AND THREAD, not invent and translate, and the i18n gate is a no-op unless a new key is
   added. It also means the cheapest correct surface is the card that already renders them, which is what Target scheme clause 2 names — CORRECTED by WA-c 2026-09-13: that card had NO production producer since `bcc1e79c3` (C3.381 moved the gate to the tool), so DA1.4 MINTED one rather than threading onto a live surface, and it cannot be raised informationally (`hasPendingDpAuthRequest` requires a matching `managed.dpAuthReplay` envelope), which is why clause 2's single button REQUIRES the envelope.
2. **The "outdated floor" code is REFUTED.** `packages/shared/src/auth/dp/dp-binary.ts` declares `DP_MIN_VERSION` as
   warn-only by 00-context decision 5 and raises no error, so an outdated-dp reason must be MINTED as a ninth code —
   and then the locale pair IS owed — or declined with the reason written down.
3. **Render it ONCE, for all three runtimes.** `packages/shared/src/agent/claude-sdk-error-mapper.ts` is claude-only;
   `attemptAuthRetry` and the typed-error dispatch in `SessionManager` are the shared seam. Building the reason line
   in the mapper produces exactly the silent divergence AGENTS.md names as this fork's largest defect class — the same
   failed re-mint named on claude and anonymous on Pi and codex. P3.333 (`731bfa630`) helps rather than blocks:
   `capturedApiError` is no longer structurally null on claude turns.

**Two compositions, neither of them optional.** (a) Coverage row `C3.381` LANDED (`bcc1e79c3`): the dp gate moved from
the TURN to the TOOL, and the 1.37.0 rig day measured the lapse as PER-RUNTIME — only the Pi card's stored key
refuses, while claude and codex authenticate from the JWT prefill. So a `NeedsLogin` reason on the gateway 401 card is
now the JWT-re-mint case only, and its wording must AGREE with that row's notice rather than compete with it; two
sentences for one cause is the defect `C3.347` records. (b) A `Timeout` reason is only honest once DA1.6 raises the
dp budgets per Target scheme clause 4 — rendering "dp timed out" against a budget smaller than dp's own startup hook
names the wrong cause.
**Tests:** each mapped code renders its reason key; an unmapped or absent code falls back to today's generic banner;
one arm per runtime if the shared seam is used.
**Native mechanism:** none — no runtime can observe a corporate `dp` exec. Host tier 2: supply it once at the shared
retry seam so the residual difference between the three runtimes is uniform rather than three error mappers. The
reference is weaker than what we already ship: their `DpAuthError` values ARE the English sentences, surfaced by
throwing, with no localisation and no reason line on a 401. Two of their members (`DownloadFailed`,
`ChecksumMismatch`) are the provisioner vocabulary DA1.6 feeds back into this row.
**Commit:** `fix(auth): the gateway 401 card names why the re-mint failed`

### DA1.5 (T5): `NESSY_HOME` in the creds paths, and the deny floor FOLLOWS the relocation

**Files:** `packages/shared/src/auth/dp/creds-store.ts` (symbols `getCredsPath` and `getOauthCredsPath` — the helper
this row used to call `getCliOauthPath()` was renamed, and BOTH now join `latchedHomeDir()` rather than `homedir()`
after P3.143 `ca187edb2`, so the base to parameterise is the LATCH);
`packages/codex-responses-adapter/src/nestor-auth.ts` (symbol `defaultCredsPath`, the THIRD hand-written resolution of
the path, which stays duplicated for the cycle reason its own docblock gives — P3.168 `6e2691c46`). **T0's open
question is answered:** `apps/electron/src/main/handlers/nessy-auth.ts` symbol `readNessyGatewayJwt` already defaults
its argument to `getCredsPath()` and needs no change. So this row has exactly TWO code sites.
**Behavior:** honour `NESSY_HOME` as the directory that REPLACES `~/.nessy`, matching the semantics the file's owner
publishes (truthy gives the resolved path, else home plus `.nessy`; a leading tilde is expanded). **Diverge on TWO points and say so** (WA-a 2026-09-13; this said one): anything not ABSOLUTE is REFUSED — a RELATIVE value rather than resolved against a cwd, and a leading tilde rather than expanded — and there is no temp-dir branch, because four resolvers in packages that cannot share code must re-derive the rule identically and 'absolute or the default' is total where an expansion step is something each copy could get subtly wrong; the relative case because `process.cwd()` in
this package is the app's own directory — the same reasoning `relocatedCredentialDenyRoots` already writes down.
**Their `getGlobalNessyDir` has a THIRD branch** a two-branch summary misses (no home directory falls back to a temp
dir), and a SECOND env name lives in the same module (`NESSY_RUNTIME_DIR`, with its own documented precedence) — this
row must say whether it mirrors one name or two.
**The SECURITY consequence no row stated, and it is what turns this from S to M.** Three read-side deny anchors are
spelled `~/.nessy` and NONE of them follows a relocation: `packages/session-tools-core/src/runtime/credential-deny-roots.ts`
symbol `credentialDenyRoots` joins home plus `.nessy`; `packages/shared/src/agent/mode-types.ts` carries the STATIC
`~/.nessy/**` glob, which is the only read-side protection with isolation off (P6.674 `e6296c0f7`); and
`packages/shared/src/agent/mode-manager.ts` symbol `relocatedCredentialDenyRoots`, which exists precisely to follow
relocated credential directories, has entries for the config-dir and XDG names and NONE for `NESSY_HOME` (P6.678
`6a9eb4869`). Shipping the variable without extending all three turns it into a switch that moves the gateway JWT out
of every floor. **Done means the floor FOLLOWS the relocation**, not merely that the interaction is stated.
**Correction to the twin `A3.10`:** "a cli-only user, a relocated home, or a test rig cannot point the credential
store anywhere" overstates the absence — a launch-time `HOME` relocates it today, because the latch captures at module
import and a re-latch helper is exported for tests. What is absent is relocation WITHOUT moving the home, plus the NAME
parity that lets the cli seed be found when `NESSY_HOME` is set.
**Target scheme clause 5** is the headless half of the same story and is owned by DA1.15 and DA1.19.
**Tests:** env set moves both paths; unset leaves the defaults; the three deny anchors follow a relocated base.
**Native mechanism:** rung 1 is the file owner's own env name — the credential file's shape is nessy-cli's contract,
and their fork-patch note for the managed-dp placeholder states in one sentence that the dp binary AND the credentials
follow `NESSY_HOME` through their storage helper, which is the strongest evidence this row has. On codex, `A3.4`'s
`cliAuthCredentialsStore` is the runtime-native credential-store lever and owes a verdict before a second host
mechanism is built beside it.
**Commit:** `fix(auth): creds paths honor NESSY_HOME like nessy-cli`

### DA1.6 (T6): managed dp — resolution ladder + lazy pinned provisioner (DP-1 amended)

**Target scheme clauses 3 and 4 govern two parts of this row:** the user's `~/.dp` session is SHARED on every rung and
is never replaced by an isolated workdir, and BOTH dp exec budgets go to 30 s.

**Files:** `packages/shared/src/auth/dp/dp-binary.ts` (the seam), `dp-runtime.ts`, `dp-api.ts` (the execs),
`session-broker.ts`, a new `dp-provision.ts`, `index.ts`; `packages/shared/src/mcp/builtin-dp-servers.ts` (the
materializer); `packages/shared/src/agent/backend/internal/runtime-resolver.ts` (symbol `resolvePluginDpRuntime`, the
write-grant alignment); the `packages/server-core/src/bootstrap/host-sandbox-settings.ts` caller; tests.

**Measured basis (2026-08-23, verifier-confirmed; re-verify only if contradicted):**
- `/usr/local/bin` is root-owned and not user-writable — `rename()` needs directory write, so `dp update` on a
  corp-installed dp is structurally impossible for a non-root user; the corp channel replaces it IN PLACE.
- `DP_WORKDIR` relocates the WHOLE state root: `~/.dp` holds the login AND the plugin registry AND the plugin
  binaries. nessy-cli's isolated workdir has an EMPTY plugin directory and no registry — an isolated workdir cannot
  serve `dp ai mcp <plugin>`, and it forces a second login. **This is Target scheme clause 3.**
- The download endpoint answers GET only (a HEAD 404s), serves arbitrary versions including the current one, and has
  NO arm64 asset — Apple Silicon runs dp under Rosetta. A runtime-written dp is unsigned and unquarantined, and
  executes; nessy-cli's copy is field proof.

**SANDBOX RE-BASING, 2026-09-12 — rewrite these sentences, do not delete the conclusion.**
`packages/shared/src/config/sandbox-settings.ts` symbol `SANDBOX_ENABLED_DEFAULT` is `false` by standing owner
decision, so `host-sandbox-settings.ts` symbol `withDpStateRootWriteGrant` — which still refuses a state root inside a
denied root, verbatim — binds only where an operator turns the sandbox on. DP-1's revocation of `~/.nessy/dp_latest`
SURVIVES on a stronger, sandbox-independent basis that post-dates it: `~/.nessy/**` is in the default `deniedPaths`,
the permission-layer floor that bites with isolation off. Both `<CONFIG_DIR>/dp/bin` and `<CONFIG_DIR>/dp/state`
survive both floors — the sandbox floor takes only `<configDir>/credentials.enc` from the config dir, and `deniedPaths`
has no `~/.nessy-agent/**` entry. **The twin `A3.13` needs the same re-basing**; its note still attributes the
revocation to the sandbox alone.

**Behavior:**
1. **Seam widens, stays sync, never execs** (the module contract is the never-runs-dp docblock in
   `builtin-dp-servers.ts`; FOUR test files depend on it at HEAD, not three):
   `resolveDpRuntime(): { dpPath; workdir?; source }`, with `resolveDpBinary()` a thin wrapper. The download lives in
   a separate async `ensureManagedDp()`, never in the resolver.
2. **Resolution ladder** (first runnable wins, `accessSync(X_OK)` only): (1) an explicit path override →
   (2) system dp (PATH, then the fallback path) unless a cached probe says below-floor or unrunnable →
   (3) `<CONFIG_DIR>/dp/bin/dp` → (4) nessy-cli's copy, **read-only: never updated, never deleted, tolerant of
   ENOENT and ETXTBSY mid-exec**, because their own auth failure path DELETES it → (5) provision into rung 3 →
   (6) `DpNotFound`. Needs-login never descends the ladder.
   **RENAME RUNG 1 BEFORE BUILDING IT.** `NESSY_DP_PATH` occurs in ZERO files of nessy-cli. The owner of that surface
   publishes `NESSY_DP` — a `${NESSY_DP}` placeholder resolvable in an MCP server's `command`, defaulting to their
   `getDpFilePath()` with a user-set `process.env.NESSY_DP` winning — landed by their `a7b7e2374` (AIDT-7054) and
   documented in their `docs/fork-patches/mcp-managed-dp-placeholder.md`. Per §READ-do-not-INVENT, take that name or
   record why we decline it; and cite their `getDpFilePath()` for rung 4 rather than hand-spelling the path.
3. **Workdir: share the system session.** `workdir` is undefined — so dp uses its own `~/.dp` — whenever `~/.dp`
   exists, on EVERY rung; the already-logged-in user never re-authenticates and the plugin registry stays intact.
   Only when `~/.dp` is absent: create `<CONFIG_DIR>/dp/state` at 0700. NEVER `~/.nessy/dp_latest`. dp's state is not
   schema-gated, and skew is bounded because the ladder never picks a binary older than the one already using the
   state. **Target scheme clause 3 makes this non-negotiable.**
4. **Every exec and spawn carries the pair.** `dp-api.ts`'s execs pass no `DP_WORKDIR` today — all become derived from
   the one record; the materializer and `resolvePluginDpRuntime` consume the SAME `resolveDpRuntime()` result rather
   than pairing a binary with a state root independently. Align `resolvePluginDpRuntime`'s `writableRoots` with
   `withDpStateRootWriteGrant`'s deny check — it grants the state root today with no deny check at all. Keep `bin/`
   out of every write grant: a sandboxed agent must not rewrite the binary it executes.
5. **Provisioner** (`dp-provision.ts`): lazy, fired only on a real dp need (a broker ensure or probe, or a pool-sync
   materialize that finds dp unavailable), never at startup, gated behind the user action that means "I want dp";
   a new installing status on the existing status channel. Atomic install, their shape: a pid-suffixed temp name →
   **sha256 plus byteLength** verify → chmod 0700 → a liveness run → rename; failure removes the temp and never
   touches a live binary; single-flight; and a **negative cache** for a downloaded-but-unrunnable version, or
   arm64-without-Rosetta becomes an infinite download loop. **No `dp update`, ever — pin-only**: their own doc admits
   the pin is inapplicable after a self-update, and dp's update channel is a second unpinned host. Freshness is a
   bumped pin at close-out, with the sha256 computed from the asset at pin-bump time. HTTPS, exact host, redirect
   error, repo TLS and CA plumbing, no proxy-CA bypass.
6. **Version floor and probe cache.** `DP_MIN_VERSION` stays warn-only for usability and becomes a ladder-preference
   input. **Key the probe cache on path plus mtime plus size** — the corp channel replaces the binary IN PLACE, so a
   path-only key misses it, and `session-broker.ts`'s cache is exactly that today. **STALE-ASSERTION HAZARD, fix it in
   the same commit:** that cache's docblock asserts the premise this row refutes, "a dp that was replaced, removed or
   upgraded resolves elsewhere and has to be re-probed" — false for the dominant case, and no gate can see it.
7. **Vocabulary.** Provisioner failure codes (download blocked, checksum mismatch, will not run on this machine) join
   DA1.4's reason line. Codes only.
8. **Packaging.** A runtime download into the user's config dir — NO electron-builder, fastlane or CI change, and
   deliberately NOT the bundled-SDK-binary pattern with its five mirrored build scripts.
9. **BUDGETS — Target scheme clause 4.** Raise BOTH `dp` exec budgets to 30 s: `dp-api.ts`'s print-token budget and
   `dp-binary.ts`'s version budget. Coverage row `C3.391` carries the measurement and the donor commit; **its note
   currently EXEMPTS the version probe and that exemption is FALSE** — our `getDpVersion` execs the SUBCOMMAND
   `version`, not the `--version` flag, and the donor's own comment draws the line at subcommand-versus-flag. Raise
   the DOCBLOCK sentences with the constants; a stale docblock beside a changed constant is the half-true disclosure
   this repo calls its largest defect class.

**TWO LIVE DEFECTS inside this row's own file set, closed only incidentally by item 4 — name them so the fix is not
narrowed.** (a) `packages/shared/src/auth/dp/dp-api.ts` symbols `printToken` and `login` exec dp with no
`HOME: latchedHomeDir()` — so the process that MINTS the token follows a confirmed managed HOME live while
`getCredsPath()` stays latched, which is the exact split `dpRuntimeEnvironment`'s own docblock says it exists to
prevent, on the one path that does not use it. Under a mid-session managed HOME the token is minted against the NEW
home's dp state and stored at the OLD home's path. (b) The 5 s print-token budget is item 9 above.

**Tests:** the ladder table-driven via injected fs predicates (no exec, no network) including below-floor descent and
`source` labels; workdir — `~/.dp` present gives undefined on every rung, absent gives the config-dir state root, with
an explicit negative for `~/.nessy/dp_latest`; sandbox — the denied state root refused with a diagnostic, the config-dir
one granted; exec env carries `DP_WORKDIR` only when the record has one; the materializer and the plugin binding get
the same command and workdir in ONE assertion; provisioner — checksum mismatch removes the temp and leaves the live
binary untouched, unrunnable is negative-cached so a second call downloads nothing, concurrent callers share one
inflight, a config dir with a space works, and an exec spy proves `dp update` is never spawned; static — one production
seam body, and `resolveDpRuntime` is not async.
**Native mechanism:** dp's own `dp update` IS the runtime's mechanism and is STRUCTURALLY unavailable to a non-root
user on a corp-installed binary — the measured cause — and the reference's own doc admits its pin is inapplicable
after a self-update. Rung 1 of native-first is exhausted BY MEASUREMENT, not skipped, and that sentence belongs in the
commit.
**Commit:** `feat(auth): managed dp copy with a resolution ladder and pinned provisioner`

### DA1.7 (T7): pin the adapter `opts.jwt` guard-bypass seam

**Files:** `packages/codex-responses-adapter/src/__tests__/` (a new unit test);
`packages/shared/src/agent/__tests__/` (a static scan).
**Behavior:** `packages/codex-responses-adapter/src/server.ts` symbol `resolveGatewayJwt` prefers an injected
`opts.jwt` over `readNestorJwt()`, which bypasses the shape guard — safe today only because no production caller
passes it (both `startAdapter` call sites, in `claude-agent.ts` and `codex-agent.ts`, pass none; the only other caller
is a reliability probe script and is not production). PIN IT: (1) a unit test naming today's behaviour — an injected
non-JWT reaches `Nestor-Token` verbatim; (2) a static scan asserting the two production call sites pass no `jwt` key,
scoped to those two files BY NAME rather than by a tree grep.
**PIN IT, DO NOT GUARD IT, and the census decides.** `jwt: '<literal>'` occurs 71 times across 13 adapter test files;
only 2 are JWT-shaped and 55 are the single character `j`. So the row's alternative branch — "or the option
documented and guarded" — costs 69 call-site edits where the pin costs one test plus one scan.
**And that same census BOUNDS the row's value honestly.** Because 69 of the 71 injected values are non-JWT-shaped,
adding the guard would redden 13 files at once — the seam cannot regress silently. This row NAMES an undocumented
behaviour; it does not close an otherwise-invisible exposure, and saying so is what keeps the XS honest.
**Why it matters more than when it was written:** the 2026-09-12 rig day measured the dp lapse as PER-RUNTIME — only
the Pi card's stored key refuses, while claude and codex authenticate from the JWT prefill — so this gate is the
credential path that carries two of three runtimes; and DA1.12 adds EXPIRY to `readNestorJwt`, which this same seam
would bypass. Land the two in one wave.
**Native mechanism:** none — the adapter is ours and the seam is host-owned. The nearest documented neighbour is
codex's `cliAuthCredentialsStore` (`A3.4`), which governs codex's own store and not this loopback gate.
**Commit:** `test(adapter): pin the jwt-injection seam against shape-guard bypass`

### DA1.8 (T8): `credentials.md` states the real rewrite behaviour

**Files:** `docs-site/content/reference/config/credentials.md` (the sentence sits under the gateway sign-in
troubleshooting heading); optionally its Russian twin.
**Behavior:** replace "Nessy Desktop never deletes or rewrites that file on its own" with the measured behaviour:
never DELETES (`clearCreds` is exported and re-exported and has no production caller), and REWRITES — atomically,
merge-preserving unknown keys — on a successful sign-in or re-mint **and** whenever a valid token is adopted from
nessy-cli's `oauth_creds.json`. **The seed clause is not pedantry:** the page tells the user to clear the file by
hand, and a nessy-cli login they would not call a sign-in here re-creates it with no dp exec — which is the shape of
the 2026-08-23 user case. Both writes are `saveCreds` call sites in `packages/shared/src/auth/dp/index.ts`.
**CROSS-PRODUCT NOTE to carry into the page or the row, discovered 2026-09-12:** nessy-cli's DLH telemetry now
resolves its token from this same file and, when it is absent or expired, runs a FULL interactive login from a
background flush. So a stale or absent creds file is a CLI-side browser-login trigger on the user's machine, not only
our own needs-login. **We ship no DLH client and must not add one** — the transferable fact is that our `saveCreds`
output is now a dependency of the other product.
**DOCS-PARITY RESIDUE, named rather than silently left:** the Russian twin had no gateway sign-in troubleshooting section when this was written. CORRECTED by WA-e 2026-09-13: `ad4f23640` (the 1.38.0 close-out commit, after the refresh `a25f77a3b`) added `### Устранение проблем со входом в шлюз` — one paragraph carrying no false sentence; what it lacked was the 401-loop paragraph, the three-command block and the by-hand-delete step, so the residue was a parity gap of three paragraphs, brought to parity by `842c95828`.
**Two corrections owed to the twin `A3.17`:** it states this defect INVERTED (the document says the file is NEVER
rewritten; the code rewrites it on two paths), and it names a shipping path that does not exist — this page ships
through `docs-site/`, and no file of that name sits under the electron resources docs directory.
**Land it with DA1.18**, which adds the troubleshooting table to the same page.
**Commit:** `docs: credentials.md states the real rewrite behavior`

**Three residues, and they are the row's actual deliverable — three sentences plus two hand-offs, not a rehearsal:**

   it cannot succeed; there is no Linux installer at all, and the macOS installer script refuses non-Darwin outright.

Different GitLab project, so its egress allowance is evidence and not authority. The comment also

### DA1.10 (T10): the Pi-side JWT scope narrows from the CLI prefix to the named gateway endpoints

**Files:** `packages/shared/src/auth/nestor.ts` (symbol `isNestorCliBaseUrl`, a prefix match);
`packages/shared/src/unified-network-interceptor.ts` (symbol `rewriteNestorAuthHeaders`);
`packages/shared/src/agent/pi-agent.ts` (symbol `resolveNestorGatewayJwtForPi`); tests in
`packages/shared/src/__tests__/unified-network-interceptor.nestor-auth.test.ts`.
**Behavior:** the adapter side enforces whole-URL equality for the gateway; the Pi side matches the whole CLI prefix —
the only path on which the dp JWT can leave the declared boundary, since a BYO `pi_compat` connection pointed at any
other path under that prefix gets the dp JWT as its apiKey and its own Bearer rewritten to `Nestor-Token`. Narrow the
CREDENTIAL sites to the NAMED endpoints. The named set today is exactly TWO URLs: the openai-like chat base and the
models URL. **`/quota` is NOT among them** — the quota URL is built from the connection ORIGIN and sits outside the
CLI prefix, carrying its own token, so the interceptor narrowing does not touch it, but narrowing `isNestorCliBaseUrl`
itself DOES, through `pi-agent.ts` symbol `describeQuota`.
**SCOPE CORRECTED — this is the row's whole risk, and it is larger than three files.** The prefix now gates **12
executable production sites across 7 modules**, and TWO of them move the OPPOSITE way under narrowing:
- `unified-network-interceptor.ts` symbol `rewriteCredentialHeader` uses the prefix to EXCLUDE ("Never on the verified
  gateway prefix"), so narrowing it would START rewriting Bearer to `x-api-key` on gateway-host paths it currently
  leaves alone — a LEAK introduced by a "narrow everything" sweep.
- `unified-network-interceptor.ts` symbol `isProviderApiUrl` uses it to WIDEN the boundary operator headers are
  allowed on; narrowing SHRINKS a documented allowance.
- FOUR sites in `packages/shared/src/telemetry/gateway-headers.ts`, whose symbol `operatorHeadersRideOnUpstream`
  declares the prefix scope DELIBERATE — "only a BYO endpoint that happens to sit under the verified prefix rides too,
  which is the same test the header stamp makes, so the two can never disagree." A mechanical sweep reverses a written
  decision without naming it.
- `packages/codex-responses-adapter/src/nestor-client.ts` hand-duplicates the prefix constant and builds the chat URL
  from it — a fourth copy of the URL-string family, and the source of one of the two named endpoints.
- Plus `model-fetchers/nestor.ts` symbol `isNestorConnection`, `backend/composer-capabilities.ts`, and a renderer icon
  helper.
**So the row is NOT "narrow seven sites or mint a second predicate": it is narrow the TWO credential sites, and state
PER SITE why each of the others keeps the prefix.**
**Tests:** keep the two existing positives green; add the MISSING negative — an UNKNOWN path under the CLI prefix
leaves `Authorization` untouched and reads no dp creds. The file's only same-host negative today sits OUTSIDE the
prefix, so it does not cover this. Mirror the negative on `resolveNestorGatewayJwtForPi`.
**Native mechanism:** none — no runtime owns "which of my URLs may carry the corporate token"; this is a host
governance predicate and belongs beside `isNessyGatewayBaseUrl` (native-first step 2). The reference is a
counter-example, and the causal chain is worth stating correctly: their provider classifies true on the auth TYPE and
on an absent baseUrl BEFORE its substring test ever runs, and attaches the token on the auth type — so they narrow
nothing and attach by identity regardless of URL. Copying it would widen our exposure.
**Twin:** `A3.16` (the wide half); DA1.11 is the narrow half of the same pair and they fail in opposite directions.
**Commit:** `fix(auth): the Pi gateway JWT attaches only to named gateway endpoints`

### DA1.11 (T11): a gateway-host endpoint that is not the gateway URL must say so

**Files:** the connection-setup validation path (`packages/server-core/src/handlers/rpc/llm-connections.ts` and
`packages/server-core/src/domain/connection-setup-logic.ts`), the gateway 401 card path, i18n; a test pinning the
Pi-prefix versus adapter-whole-URL asymmetry.
**Behavior (D2 R1 — the leading cause of the 2026-08-23 user case):** a `claude-runtime`, `codex-runtime` or
`pi_compat` connection whose baseUrl is on the corp gateway HOST but fails `isNessyGatewayBaseUrl`'s whole-URL match
silently gets NO `Nestor-Token` (BYO classification, Bearer only) AND no forced re-mint — `SessionManager` symbol
`refreshGatewayAuthForRetry` computes `gatewayBacked` from the SAME predicate and returns early — so the two failures
compound and the gateway's raw token-parse 401 reads as a credential problem. **The whole-URL boundary is deliberate
and must NOT be widened:** it is derived from the chat URL constant precisely so transport and classification cannot
disagree. The fix is a DETECTOR plus an honest surface: at connection save and on the 401 card, "this endpoint is on
the corp gateway host but is not the gateway URL; corp tokens are not attached to it — use the one-click gateway card."
**The near-miss is already guarded and the gap already written down:**
`apps/electron/src/renderer/components/apisetup/byo-runtime.ts` refuses the EXACT gateway URL in both
`validateByoRuntimeForm` and `validateMergedEndpointForm`, and its docblock states this row's gap in its own words —
"the same host on a *different* path stays allowed: per AGENTS.md that is a custom endpoint with normal provider auth."
**BIGGEST CORRECTION, and it re-prices the row: there are already FIVE implementations of "is this the gateway" (WA-0 2026-09-13; this said THREE — the fifth, `packages/shared/src/config/llm-connections.ts` symbol `isNessyGatewayConnectionShape`, ALREADY implements the parsed-HOST class this row says needs building, both arms pinned in `gateway-only-connections.test.ts`; REUSE it, do not mint), and
the one that runs at the SAVE-TIME surface this row targets is NOT the shared one.**
`apps/electron/src/renderer/components/apisetup/nessy-gateway.ts` declares its own `isNessyGatewayBaseUrl` and
`isNessyGatewayConnection` over a HAND-WRITTEN base-URL literal plus its own normaliser; `apps/electron/src/main/handlers/nessy-auth.ts` symbol `isNessyGatewayConnection` is the third (the gateway-login-check module this sentence used to name is GONE at HEAD; only its test kept the old name); and `AiSettingsPage.tsx` documents WHY the renderer cannot import the
shared one (its barrel re-exports node-only agent code no renderer module imports). So "extend `classifyModelTarget`'s
verdict union rather than mint a second predicate" — the right instinct, since parity P3.363 made
`upstream-target.ts` the one module for target governance — **cannot serve the save-time notice without a fourth copy or a module split. Price that before writing the class. — CORRECTED by WA-d 2026-09-13: that holds for `classifyModelTarget` (under `agent/backend/`, whose barrel re-exports node-only code) and NOT for the predicate the row reuses — `packages/shared/src/config/llm-connections.ts` symbol `isNessyGatewayConnectionShape` is reachable from the renderer through the `@config` alias (`AiSettingsPage.tsx` and `CompactModelSelector.tsx` already import that module as a VALUE); the detector cost one import and one nine-line function (`4bee28184`).**
**IN-ZONE FIX, in the same commit:** the renderer twin's docblock claims it is "kept in sync by the shared
`NESSY_GATEWAY_BASE_URL` literal", and that name is declared ONLY in that renderer file. There is no shared literal;
the backend derives from the chat URL and the renderer hand-writes it. The docblock is false.
**The constant a gateway-host class needs ALREADY EXISTS and is exported:** `packages/shared/src/auth/nestor.ts` symbol `NESTOR_GATEWAY_HOST`, parsed from `NESTOR_CLI_BASE_PREFIX`. Note the duplication before reusing it (WA-0 2026-09-13): `packages/server-core/src/model-fetchers/nestor.ts` declares its OWN `NESTOR_GATEWAY_HOST` as a hand-written literal and builds ITS prefix from that — the two files derive in opposite directions and both spell the host by hand; the same class as this row's own in-zone fix (a two-lane change: WA-c's fetcher, WA-d's `auth/nestor.ts`).
Keep P3.363's own rule that a host is PARSED and never substring-matched.
**Tests:** the asymmetry test this row asks for does NOT exist. `runtime-switch.test.ts`'s same-host negative pins the
CONTEXT-WINDOW tier, and the two runtime suites each pin one side ("treats the gateway host with a different path as
BYO, not as Nestor") with nothing asserting the Pi side answers the opposite way on purpose. Add it. — SUPERSEDED by WA-d 2026-09-13: since `a4f36eaea` (DA1.10) the Pi CREDENTIAL side answers the SAME way as the adapter side, so that opposition is closed; the new test pins the four-way answer on one URL instead — wide `isNestorCliBaseUrl` TRUE, `isNestorGatewayCredentialUrl` FALSE, `isNessyGatewayBaseUrl` FALSE, HOST class TRUE.
**Native mechanism:** none — no runtime can answer "is this endpoint my gateway"; the host answers it once for all
