### DA1.12 (T12): the adapter creds gate honours expiry, with its own 401 code

**Target scheme clause 1 makes this row load-bearing:** the credential of record is the JWT in the creds file, and an
EXPIRED one is precisely the re-mint trigger that clause names. A gate that reports it as "not found" sends the user
to the wrong remedy.

**Files:** `packages/codex-responses-adapter/src/nestor-auth.ts` (symbol `readNestorJwt` — shape only today; the
string `expiresAt` does not occur anywhere in the file); `packages/codex-responses-adapter/src/server.ts` (symbol
`resolveGatewayJwt` and the single not-found constant rendered at both refusal sites); tests.
**Behavior:** the Claude and Codex arms post an expired-but-well-shaped JWT indefinitely — only the one-shot
`attemptAuthRetry` ever re-mints — because `readNestorJwt` checks SHAPE while `packages/shared/src/auth/dp/creds-store.ts`
symbol `getValidCreds` checks shape AND `expiresAt`, over a field the shape's owner documents as epoch ms. Duplicate
the three-line expiry rule into `nestor-auth.ts` under the cycle-avoidance comment already there — the package must
stay free of `@nessy-agent/shared`, which is why `isJwtShaped` and the creds path are already hand-written — and add a
gate 401 code DISTINCT from the not-found one ("gateway credentials expired") on BOTH routes, so DA1.4 has a reason to
render and the user is told to sign in again rather than that their API key may be invalid.
**TWO SITES the row's done-means would otherwise leave behind.**
1. `packages/shared/src/agent/codex-agent.ts` symbol `queryLlm` calls `readNestorJwt()` DIRECTLY and throws a THIRD
   copy of the not-found sentence. The expiry half reaches it for free; the distinct-code half does not, and a test set
   scoped to "both wires" misses it.
2. `resolveGatewayJwt` prefers an injected `opts.jwt`, so an expiry check placed only inside `readNestorJwt` is
   BYPASSED by any caller that injects. No production caller does today — which is why this is a sequencing note and
   not a hole — but landing this row FIRST makes DA1.7's unpinned seam load-bearing for an expiry guarantee. Land the
   two in one wave.
**Tests:** (pos) a shaped jwt with a future expiry passes; (neg) the same jwt with a past expiry is refused with the
EXPIRED code on both routes; (neg) a missing file still yields the not-found code. **One existing case must be
UPDATED rather than left:** the adapter's units suite writes a shaped jwt with `expiresAt: 1` — epoch ms 1, i.e. 1970 —
and asserts the jwt comes back. That assertion is today's proof the expiry is unread, and it becomes tomorrow's
negative.
**Native mechanism:** none to adopt — neither the Claude SDK nor codex owns this file. The contract is nessy-cli's
(their token manager returns creds only while the expiry is in the future, and their delegated-token reader refuses a
past `exp`), so we are implementing the reader the file's owner already specifies. That is duplication of a known
rule, not invention.
**Twin:** `A3.8`.
**Commit:** `fix(adapter): the creds gate refuses an expired JWT with its own code`

### DA1.13 (T13): the gateway card stops carrying a stale credential snapshot

**Files:** `packages/server-core/src/handlers/rpc/llm-connections.ts` (the setup-RPC write
`manager.setLlmApiKey(setup.slug, setup.credential)`);
`packages/shared/src/agent/backend/internal/drivers/upstream-target.ts` (symbol `attachUpstreamCredential`, the
remaining consumer after DA1.2 and DA1.3). Design note first, then implement.
**Behavior (D2 N4):** the gateway card's stored "API key" is a sign-in-time JWT snapshot. **Lifecycle REFINED by
measurement:** it is written at card creation AND at any RE-RUN of the one-click card, which resolves the existing
gateway slug and upserts through the same setup RPC — and never by a background re-mint, never by Reconnect, whose
handler says so in its own comment ("Clear «Not authenticated» in place — no navigation, no re-creation"). The
verifier's refutation of "click Sign in to refresh it" therefore STANDS, with that one honest exception named.
**THREE CARDS, not one.** `apps/electron/src/renderer/components/apisetup/gateway-one-click.ts` symbol
`buildGatewaySetup` sets `credential: jwt` for the claude AND codex gateway cards as well as for the Pi card, and
`ApiKeyInput.tsx` additionally PREFILLS the field from the same bridge when the gateway preset is active. So the blast
radius is three stored JWT snapshots with no lifecycle, one per gateway card.
**WRITER CENSUS CORRECTED:** there are TWO production writers, not one. Beside the setup RPC,
`packages/shared/src/agent/backend/factory.ts` symbol `testBackendConnection` writes under a transient test slug and
deletes it in a top-level `finally` whose swallowed catch is the one path that can leave a key behind. A row whose
deliverable is "one written rule for when the snapshot is created, read, refreshed and discarded" cannot be written
against a census missing a writer.
**PREFER REMOVAL.** On a correctly classified gateway connection NOTHING reads its VALUE — but three presence readers take the card away on its ABSENCE (`LIST_WITH_STATUS`'s `isAuthenticated`, `factory.ts` symbol `validateStoredBackendConnection`, `CredentialManager.checkHealth`), so removal is a PAIR with a presence arm, not a deletion (WA-c 2026-09-13, `f4b57140b`); and the writer census missed a third door — `ApiKeyInput.tsx` PREFILLS the JWT on the gateway preset, so the refusal had to be server-side. The value half: `attachUpstreamCredential` returns
early unless the target is openai-compatible, and the anthropic driver declines by name — "Reading them here would add
a second reader of a file this repo keeps to one." `canEditLlmConnection` already hides the Edit form for gateway
connections precisely so the stored JWT is not revealed, and the shape's owner keeps exactly ONE copy of the
credential and reads it live.
**SEQUENCING IS PART OF THE ROW NOW.** The row's premise "T2/T3 remove two consumers" is UNFULFILLED — both are open
at HEAD — so removal must land WITH or AFTER DA1.2 and DA1.3, or it silently changes what those two paths send. If
this row lands FIRST, DA1.2 and DA1.3 shrink from guards to deletions; decide that at DA0.1 rather than executing
three rows against one object in an arbitrary order.
**Migration:** existing gateway connections hold a key in the OS keychain under their slug — delete it rather than
orphan it — and the Pi card's slug is shared with user-created custom endpoints, so the migration must target
CONFIRMED gateway connections only, using the same confirmation the one-click path already performs before upserting.
**One owner sentence to RECONCILE at review, not to assume:** Target scheme clause 1 says the gateway credential is
the JWT in `~/.nessy/dp_auth_creds.json`, read live. Read against the tree that is what the claude and codex arms
already do and what removal would give Pi — it is not a mandate to keep a per-connection copy as the authority.
**Native mechanism:** none to adopt; the host owns the connection store, and the reference shape is the single
credential store nessy-cli keeps.
**Twin:** `A3.9` (which owns DA1.2 as well), and `A3.7` measures the same snapshot from the model-fetcher side.
**Commit:** `fix(auth): the gateway card stops carrying a stale credential snapshot`

### DA1.14 (NEW, nessy-cli delta): the 401/403 re-mint classifier asymmetry

**Born 2026-09-12** from a read of the nessy-cli delta. **REFERENCE, do not duplicate: the seam is already owned by
`A3.6`, `A3.7` and `A3.12`, and by DA1.4 above; the 403 arm is a two-line addition inside a lane that exists.**
**Files:** `packages/server-core/src/sessions/SessionManager.ts` (symbol `messageLooksLikePlainAuthError` and the
typed-code branch beside it).
**Behavior:** their content generator's auth classifier fires on 401 AND 403 and on five message substrings; ours
classifies no 403 anywhere — the typed branch tests two codes, neither 403-shaped, and the plain-text branch matches
only expired-token, sign-in-again and a 401-plus-unauthorized pairing. Add the 403 and forbidden arm to the PLAIN-TEXT
branch (the typed branch has no 403-shaped code to test today), plus the negative control that a fault report
containing "forbidden" still fails the surfaced-fault gate and therefore cannot re-send the user's message.
**CARRY THE NULL.** No 403 has been MEASURED on the gateway: `packages/server-core/src/model-fetchers/nestor.ts`
records 401 for a wrong header and 5xx for an unparseable credential. This row closes a classifier asymmetry with the
donor; it does not reproduce a failure, and the row says so rather than implying a measurement.
**DO NOT COPY THE DONOR'S RETRY.** At their HEAD the retry mutates only the pipeline client's apiKey while the request
header was computed once at client build — so on the classic `Nestor-Token` path their retry replays the STALE JWT.
Our research base already records that as their live bug. The concept transfers; the mechanism does not.
**Size:** S. **Native mechanism:** none — error classification of a corporate gateway is host work.
**Commit:** `fix(auth): a 403 from the gateway is an auth failure too`

### DA1.15 (NEW, nessy-cli delta): the Spirit-IAM delegated-token env name and its semantics

**Born 2026-09-12. Target scheme clause 5: align or DOCUMENT. MEASURE-FIRST — the risk is untested, not proven.**
**Files:** `packages/shared/src/auth/dp/env-token.ts` (symbols `NESSY_DP_AUTH_TOKEN_ENV` and `tryEnvToken`);
`packages/shared/src/auth/dp/exchange.ts` (symbol `exchangeDpToken`).
**The two shapes.** Theirs: env `NESSY_CLI_DP_AUTH_TOKEN`, DECODED as an already-issued Spirit-IAM JWT (expiry
checked, never exchanged, seeded only in memory), and carried as `Authorization: Bearer` by VALUE-EQUALITY against
that env var — because the gateway's oathkeeper runs a `spirit_iam` JWKS authenticator BESIDE the token-introspection
one. Ours: env `NESSY_DP_AUTH_TOKEN`, EXCHANGED through the token endpoint, with the divergence declared in the file's
own docblock as a plan decision — i.e. an owner call, not an oversight. Their name occurs in zero lines of
`packages/**` and `apps/**` here.
**THE RISK, stated as the hypothesis it is:** a Spirit-IAM delegated token is minted for the OTHER authenticator, and
we feed it to the exchange endpoint as if it were a dp access token. If the exchange refuses it, our headless and CI
bypass fails while the cli's identical variable works on the same machine — but **nobody has driven a real delegated
token through `exchangeDpToken`, here or anywhere in the record.** The measurement is the deliverable.
**Correction to an earlier reading:** "no `Authorization: Bearer` path exists in this tree" is FALSE as written —
`exchangeDpToken` already sends a Bearer to the exchange endpoint. What is absent is a Bearer on the CHAT request
headers.
**Options, after the measurement and not before it:** (A) honour their name with THEIR semantics — decode, do not
exchange, and introduce this tree's first Bearer branch on the chat path; S–M, and a second auth shape to test.
(B) read the name ONLY to DIAGNOSE: when theirs is set and ours is not, say so in the sign-in error and name the variable we do read; XS — CORRECTED by WA-a 2026-09-13: B SPLITS. The documentation half is XS and DONE (`79f58ad0d`, and WA-e's DA1.19 page); the sign-in-error half is S and cross-zone, because `DpAuthServiceError` carries a CODE and nothing else (every consumer reads `.code`; `GatewayAuthStatus` is `error:${DpAuthError}` by design) — it is DA1.23. **Default if unanswered: B.** Do NOT hand `exchangeDpToken` a Spirit-IAM JWT on a guess —
that is step 4 of read-do-not-invent taken before steps 1 to 3, against a divergence the file records as a plan
decision.
**Size:** M (measure), then XS or S depending on the option.
**Pairs with DA1.19**, which is the documentation half of the same headless story.
**Commit:** `fix(auth): the headless env token names the variable the user actually set`

### DA1.16 (NEW, nessy-cli delta): `/cli/models` grew `sampling_params`, `reasoning` and `extra_body`

**Born 2026-09-12. MEASURE-FIRST.**
**Files:** `packages/server-core/src/model-fetchers/nestor.ts` (symbols `NestorModel` and `fetchNestorModels`);
`packages/shared/src/config/models.ts` (symbol `ModelDefinition`) only if the measurement says it must grow a carrier.
**What moved.** Their models client now reads four new per-model fields off the backend row and folds them into a
generation config. Ours reads id, name, alias, description, the default flag and the context-window family; the
sampling field appears in this tree in exactly ONE place and it is a COMMENT recording our own 2026-08-28 probe of
that endpoint's eight field names, `extra_body` appears nowhere, and `ModelDefinition` has no carrier for any of them.
**Our own probe already SAW the field and the parser walked past it** — the same shape the nestor fetcher's own
docblock calls out for the context window, "a hard-coded fallback answering a question the API had started answering
itself."
**THE SHARP END IS `reasoning`, not the sampling knobs.** Their fork-patch states that without translation the nested
form is ignored and the server silently defaults to the highest effort; the catalogue's own coder row publishes a LOW
effort. If that is true of the gateway generally, every gateway turn that sends no effort is billed and latency-priced
at the top setting invisibly, which is the silent-divergence class. **WEAK evidence tier: that is their prose, not our
measurement.**
**THE ADAPTER HALF IS ALREADY ANSWERED, and this row is where the evidence lands.** Coverage row `C4.37` closed on
owner option A, deferring a `supportsThinking` flip "until the translating adapter is probed for a reasoning control".
Probed at HEAD, the adapter carries one END TO END: `packages/codex-responses-adapter/src/anthropic/inbound.ts` builds
a `reasoning` object from Claude Code's effort or from a thinking budget and omits it for disabled thinking, and
`packages/codex-responses-adapter/src/to-chat.ts` symbol `toChatRequest` maps it to a FLAT `reasoning_effort` (P3.265)
— which is exactly the wire form their own translation helper independently measured the Nestor models to require. So
the adapter half is answered in the affirmative and only the SERVER half remains.
**Done means:** one re-read of the endpoint recording the FULL per-row field set WITH the date (the shape has moved
twice — eight names on 2026-08-28, a reasoning block present on 2026-09-11), and one gateway turn driven with and
without a flat effort key. Only then decide whether `ModelDefinition` grows a carrier, and record an explicit verdict
on the sampling and extra-body fields — **a stated refusal beats a silent drop.**
**Fold one adjacent posture in the same file rather than opening a row for it:** `fetchNestorModels` spreads
user-authored overrides BEFORE the server's id, name, short name, description and context window, so those five are
safe and ANY OTHER user-authored key on a gateway model row survives a catalogue refresh. Their equivalent change
removed exactly this, on the grounds that the backend owns model metadata on the dp-auth provider. Either narrow the
spread to a named allowlist on gateway connections or write that the residue is deliberate.
**Size:** M. **Native mechanism:** the gateway publishes the values; the host is the only consumer. Their
`is_default` reader is the standing reason NOT to mirror their catalogue reader field for field — it declares a field
name the gateway does not publish, so their default-flag read has been dead since it was written.
**Commit:** `feat(models): the gateway's per-model generation config stops being dropped on the floor`

### DA1.17 (NEW, nessy-cli delta): `/quota` on the gateway cards, and a proactive warning — OWNER QUESTION

**Born 2026-09-12. This row is BLOCKED on an owner answer and must not dispatch without one.**
**The reading, and it is unambiguous.** Their gate is WHO the user is — the auth type — so `/quota` is available in
every mode. Ours is the RUNTIME: `packages/shared/src/agent/backend/composer-capabilities.ts` symbol
`offersNestorQuota` answers true for the nestor-cloud provider and for Pi on the gateway prefix, and the menu row is
excluded otherwise. But the two OTHER default-on gateway cards carry the same base URL and the same JWT prefill and
are metered by the SAME per-user limiter: `packages/server-core/src/domain/connection-setup-logic.ts` defines
`claude-gateway` and `codex-gateway`. `claude-agent.ts` has no `describeQuota` override, so it falls to the base
class's "Quota is not available on this runtime" — TRUE of the runtime, FALSE of the gateway budget the user is
spending.
**TWO CORRECTIONS that change the row's shape from "gap" to "re-argument".**
1. **This is not an oversight: it is a documented and TEST-PINNED decision.** `offersNestorQuota`'s own docblock says
   "the Claude Nessy Gateway card carries the same baseUrl prefix, and its runtime has no `/quota` path, so it must
   not be smuggled in by the URL alone", and
   `packages/shared/src/agent/backend/__tests__/composer-capabilities.test.ts` pins the claude-runtime gateway case as
   a NEGATIVE CONTROL. Option A flips that; the row must price the flip. The re-argument is still legitimate, and this
   is why: the recorded REASON is about the RUNTIME's own budget surface, and it is irrelevant to a host-side read
   through `describeNestorQuota`, which is provider-neutral code hanging off a provider-neutral seam whose URL
   resolver already takes only the connection's origin.
2. **Codex's DOCBLOCK names the right answer; its EMITTED message does not.** `codex-agent.ts` symbol `describeQuota`
   says in a comment that the gateway budget is the host's and the codex arm is not it — but the message a user
   actually receives ends without naming the gateway budget or where to see it. So option B is owed on BOTH gateway
   cards, not only on claude's.
**OPTIONS.**
- **(A)** Widen the gate to the gateway families and let the host answer for all three, flipping the pinned negative
  control and rewriting its docblock in the same commit. Size M. Native-first-correct: the host must make the residual
  difference UNIFORM or HONEST, and today it is neither — the same user, spending the same budget, sees it on one card
  and is told it is unavailable on another.
- **(B)** Keep the gate and fix only the SENTENCE, so the refusal names the gateway budget and where to see it, on
  both cards. Size XS, and it removes the false half immediately.
**Default per the controller's brief until the owner answers: (A) as the posture, with (B) available as the XS floor
that can land on its own.**
**SEPARATE AND ADDITIVE, size S, gated on the same answer: the PROACTIVE arm.** Their TUI reads the budget at session
start and after a completed response and shows a short notice at a warning or exhausted state. Ours is on-demand only,
and the expensive half is already built —
`packages/shared/src/agent/backend/internal/nestor-quota.ts` holds the client, the URL resolver, the formatter and the
level policy — so this is a TRIGGER plus a surface, not a new integration. **Honour that module's level policy
verbatim** (a warning level is reserved for an auth failure; a transport blip must not leave a standing banner) and
add per-state suppression so one warning is not re-announced every turn. Adjacent rows to read first rather than
re-derive: coverage `C4.28` (the codex override, closed as measured-then-refuted) and `C4.48` (an open, paused owner
row on the credential-path surface); and a parked entry in the 2026-08-22 nestor-cloud contract plan records "quota UI
beyond `/quota`" as previously deferred.
**Native mechanism:** none — no runtime publishes the gateway's budget; it is the gateway's, and the host is the only
seam. That is precisely the case native-first is written for.
**Commit (option B floor):** `fix(quota): the gateway cards name the budget they cannot show`

## Phase 2 — External-model MCP exposure

**Honest scope statement (owner-approved 2026-08-22; lands verbatim in the docs-site page at close-out), with its
2026-09-12 corrections folded in:**

1. What the whitelist promises: *a corp-proxy external model is not handed tools that act on internal systems*
   (dpJira, dpGitlab, …). It does NOT promise *internal data cannot reach an external model*: native `Bash` and `Read`
   carry no `mcp__` prefix and are not filtered, isolation is off by default (standing owner decision), and the
   conversation body itself — file contents, ticket text — is in the request before any tool is offered.
2. The whitelist alone does not unblock anthropic-on-llm-proxy: the guardrail on the messages route refuses ANY
   tool-carrying request (the measured 403 lists even our own session tools). That route needs ODR-1 and DA2.4. What
   the whitelist DOES close is the silent leak: today the full dp, plugin and session tool array rides unfiltered to
   the proxy on the Pi and codex arms, where there is no guardrail, unlogged.
3. **CORRECTED 2026-09-12 — the third deliverable of this phase is already DONE and the promise has shrunk
   accordingly.** The guardrail-403 misdiagnosis fixes landed in the parity plan (DA2.7), and the DISCLOSURE half of
   the visibility work landed with them. What Phase 2 still owes is the withholding half, and the owner deferred
   exactly that on 2026-09-05 to the plan you are reading. **Until DA2.1 settles the DP-5 collision, nothing in this
   phase may withhold a tool.**

### DA2.1 (W1): one classifier module (DP-4) — 🟨 PARTIALLY LANDED

**LANDED as parity `P3.363` (`cb02270d0`, `e8f14543e`, `731bfa630`, `89f28509e`, plus `d93109114` for the adapter
sink and a later dedup-tier fix).** The predicate exists at the file this row named:
`packages/shared/src/agent/backend/internal/drivers/upstream-target.ts` symbol `classifyModelTarget`, beside
`isNessyGatewayBaseUrl`, with `ModelTargetVerdict`, `modelTargetToolEgressNotice`, `noteModelTargetToolEgress` and a
table-driven test. **Do not rebuild it. Re-read the row against it before writing any code: three of its clauses are
refuted.**

1. **There is NO corp-proxy host list, and there must not be a naive one.**
   `apps/electron/src/renderer/components/apisetup/byo-runtime.ts` symbol `DEFAULT_ENDPOINT_URL` is the very host this
   row wanted to declare "corporate" — it is the BYO card's DEFAULT endpoint — so the list as specified restricts
   every BYO user, which DP-5 exempts by name. The shipped predicate classifies on endpoint KIND plus model
   NAMESPACE instead: the gateway (whole-URL) and loopback are unrestricted, the internal namespace is
   vendor-internal, an eight-name external-vendor namespace list is external-vendor, and everything else is
   unclassified and fails CLOSED.
2. **The verdict is SIX classes, not two, and `restricted` is ADVISORY** — the module says "Nothing in this tree
   withholds a tool on it." The name exists so the enforcement lane inherits ONE predicate instead of minting a
   second.
3. **The `network-address-guard.ts` half never landed and should be read as refuted, not as an anchor:**
   `managedAllowlist` there is the network DOMAIN allowlist consumed by `SessionManager` and the MCP pool, and it
   carries no model-target list.
**What the row asked for and DID land:** the host is PARSED and never substring-matched (with a test pinning the path
trick), the verdict carries no path, query or credential, and the predicate is pure, synchronous and cannot throw.

**The `is_internal` sentence this row already carries is TRUE at HEAD in both halves, and a "half refuted" reading of
it is withdrawn.** Our own fetcher maps id, name, alias, description, the default flag and the context window and
nothing else; the field appears in this tree in ONE place and only as prose, in a docblock recording the 2026-08-28
probe. And in the reference the field is NOT load-bearing: their models client reads it, but their model registry
OVERWRITES it one hop later with an unconditional prefix test, so what reaches their allow-check is the `tgpt/`
prefix. Reading their catalogue field as the signal would copy a value their own code discards.

**WHAT IS LEFT, and it is a DECISION before it is code. Size S.** DP-5 says BYO is unrestricted; the shipped predicate
calls a BYO endpoint AT an external vendor `external-vendor` / restricted. Those coexist only while the verdict is
advisory. Resolve it — connection KIND (gateway, BYO, subscription, loopback) as a first axis beside host and
namespace, or namespace-only permanently with the host list formally dropped — and write the resolution into the
module docblock IN THE SAME COMMIT. The landed test deliberately encodes the un-resolved state (a BYO endpoint at the
vendor reads as external-vendor and restricted) because a DISCLOSURE is right under either resolution and a
WITHHOLDING is not; changing that row is part of resolving it.
**Native mechanism:** none on any runtime is target-conditioned — the claude SDK disables its own tool-search
deferral for a non-first-party base URL, and codex's is hardcoded off — which is why this is host-side and shared.
**FILE-ZONED** against `A3.5` and `A3.16`, which both cite this module; never run them in parallel.
**Commit:** `feat(mcp): the model-target classifier answers the DP-5 collision`

### DA2.2 (W2): policy layer — the model-target pool filter, and the managed-policy de-gate — 🟨 PARTIALLY LANDED

**TWO halves with OPPOSITE verdicts. Do not execute this as one task.**

**Half B — the de-Pi-gate — is DONE. Do not re-execute it, and do not grep for `isPiCapabilityTurn`, which no longer
exists in code.** Parity `P2.314` (`acb727ae8`) replaced the Pi-only test with `isRuntimeCapabilityTurn` in
`packages/server-core/src/bootstrap/storage-runtime.ts` symbol `buildCapabilityResolutionInputsV2`, whose own comment
states the axis correction — "the distinction this gate needed was RUNTIME TURN vs BROWSE, never Pi vs everything" —
so an administrator's managed MCP and plugin lists now apply on claude-runtime, codex-runtime and nestor-cloud turns.
The row's scope warning asked for all three sub-halves to be verified DELIBERATELY, and all three are covered: MCP
through `isManagedMcpServerAllowed` at the record filter and at the plugin candidates, plugins through
`isManagedPluginAllowed`, hooks through `areManagedPluginHooksAllowed`, `isManagedAllHooksDisabled` and
`isManagedHooksOnly`, plus `P2.206`'s runtime-independent floor read in the pinned-agent automation builder.
**TWO MORE COMMITS ON THE SAME GATE the row never named, both squarely on its subject.** `P2.351` (`3353e3215`) closed
a CRITICAL: the browse early-return was letting a DENIED MCP record survive into the workspace pool registry's sync
and be SPAWNED, with its configured env and headers, on every non-ephemeral session start — the record is now KEPT for
the catalogue and stamped not-source-enabled so the runnable view excludes it. `P2.368` (`3161332d2`) closed a SECOND
denylist bypass, broader than the first: built-in dp MCP records were appended AFTER the managed filter and escaped
the denylist on every path.

**Half A — the model-target filter — is the WHOLE remaining row, and it is BLOCKED on DA2.1's decision. Size L.**
Nothing at HEAD narrows a session's MCP set by which model it is pointed at:
`packages/shared/src/capabilities/resolver.ts` interface `CapabilityContext` carries no connection, endpoint or model
field, so the input the filter needs does not reach the resolver, and no whitelist constant exists anywhere.
**Build it at the pool, not per runtime:** all three read one pool through `McpClientPool.slugsForLeaser`, which is the
same argument `P2.314` used to delete the per-provider early return. Runtime switch and model switch must re-sync the
filter, host-side, so it survives handover.
**TWO CORRECTIONS for the build.**
1. **The seed intersection is 7 of 8, not 8.** `context7` is the fourth entry of
   `packages/shared/src/mcp/builtin-dp-servers.ts` symbol `BUILTIN_DP_MCP_EXCLUSIONS`, a list of names the set
   "deliberately does not carry", with the reason recorded beside it. Seeding the constant at 8 would mint an entry
   for a server this repo has a written decision NOT to ship. The builtin set itself is 28 entries, every one
   `defaultEnabled: false` and none `true`.
2. **Session tools remain an EXPLICIT OPEN ITEM, and the precondition understates the gap.** `mcp__session__*` is not
   pool-served and rides a DEFAULT configuration, so a pool-only filter cannot clear the measured 403 — which names
   our own session tools. Their narrowing is decided with ODR-1 and DA2.4, not here. (Label that premise honestly: it
   is restated from a plan row rather than measured, and zero occurrences of a prefix in one file is not a
   positive-controlled absence proof.)
**Tests:** an external target gets no non-whitelisted server in ANY of the three runtimes' tool sets (the Pi proxy
definitions, the claude server map, the codex config blocks); an internal target is unchanged; the managed policy is
enforced on a codex turn.
**Native mechanism:** none; the reference filters at server ENABLEMENT and at per-request tool-list construction,
which is structurally the same seam half A wants. **Do NOT port its boot-time fail-open** — our landed predicate
deliberately does the opposite and fails closed on unclassified.
**Commit:** `feat(mcp): external models receive only whitelisted MCP servers`

### DA2.3 (W3): enforcement layer — the adapter filters what the policy layer cannot see

**Files:** `packages/codex-responses-adapter/src/server.ts` (symbol `handleMessages`, which has the upstream and the
model); `packages/codex-responses-adapter/src/anthropic/inbound.ts` (symbol `toolsToResponses`, the precedent — it
already drops unroutable kinds); and a NAMED policy-exception note in
`packages/codex-responses-adapter/src/anthropic/passthrough-negotiation.ts`'s doctrine. **Two anchors this row used to
carry are dead and are named here in words rather than re-spelled: the line anchor into the passthrough doctrine (the
durable reference is its `REMOVABLE_BETA_FIELDS` docblock, which names `tools` explicitly), and the line anchor into
the 2026-08-17 claude live-session narrative for the "no record" sentence (that line is now an unrelated tracker row).**
**Behavior:** defense in depth on the two adapter-served runtimes: for a restricted-classified upstream and model,
drop non-whitelisted `mcp__*` tools from the outbound body and log the removal, NAMES ONLY. Skip Pi-side wire masking
entirely — Pi is covered by DA2.2's pool narrowing, and wire masking desyncs from history, a known live failure mode.

**BUILD IT BESIDE THE OBSERVATION THAT IS ALREADY THERE, not as a second mechanism.** `P3.363` added
`AdapterOptions.onOutboundTools` and the helper `noteOutboundTools` to `server.ts`, fired at THREE sites — the
Responses route, the translated Anthropic route and the untranslated Anthropic passthrough — from the body about to be
sent, handing the upstream, the model and the tool NAMES to the host, which classifies and discloses. It withholds
nothing today. Keep two properties of that design: **names only**, because the SCHEMAS are the payload under
discussion and copying them into a log would make the disclosure a second copy of the thing disclosed; and **the
adapter classifies nothing**, because this package is the dependency ROOT of the pair and may not import the shared
package. So the filter is a HOST-SUPPLIED PREDICATE injected the way the idle-timeout reader already is.

**ONE CONSTRAINT THIS ROW DID NOT HAVE WHEN IT WAS WRITTEN, AND IT SPLITS THE ROW.**
`llm-gateway-protocol.md#feature-pass-through` says a gateway that rewrites or redacts request bodies for content
inspection breaks the pairing, so inspect without modifying; `llm-gateway-protocol.md#forward-as-open-lists`
conditions its rule on an Anthropic-format upstream, with an explicit exception for a non-Anthropic upstream "where
bridging the schema difference is the gateway's job". **That exception is our TRANSLATED arm, where the body is
rewritten anyway; it is NOT our `anthropic-messages` PASSTHROUGH arm, which is the arm both sentences are literally
about.** So a filter is defensible on the Responses and translated arms and CONTRADICTS the page on the passthrough
arm. **Decide the passthrough arm explicitly — refuse to filter it and state the residue, or filter it and cite the
exception — rather than shipping one uniform filter across three arms.** The classifier module's own docblock records
this reasoning and concludes that the honest seam for a filter is the host's own tool set, not the adapter's outbound
body; a lane that disagrees must say so in writing.
**BLOCKED TWICE, both upstream in this same phase:** on DA2.1, because today's verdict calls a BYO endpoint at a
vendor restricted and the adapter serves precisely those BYO upstreams; and on DA2.2, because there is no whitelist
constant to enforce.
**Pi stays out, and the residue is sharper than the row knew:** Pi has no `onOutboundTools` equivalent, so on the Pi
arm even the tool-NAMED disclosure is absent — `packages/shared/src/agent/backend/factory.ts` fires only the unnamed
target notice at backend construction, where the session's MCP tool set does not exist yet. If DA2.2's pool narrowing
is what covers Pi, say so in the same commit.
**Tests:** a restricted upstream gets a filtered body on both routes; gateway and BYO upstreams are untouched; the
removal is logged.
**Native mechanism:** none is target-conditioned on either adapter-served runtime, so defense in depth here is host
construction and not configuration.
**Commit:** `feat(adapter): external upstreams get the whitelist enforced at the wire`

### DA2.4 (W4): anthropic-wire tools mode — measurement-gated, see the ODR-1 branch table

**A RIG-DAY row, not a lane row: it needs the owner's entitled `claude-custom-2` credential.**
**Step 1 — the probe series, now TWO arms and not three.** Arm (i), zero tools, is ALREADY MEASURED on that rig:
`docs/testing/cdp-runs/2026-08-21-t23-lane-g.md` records the tool-free probe in the same run answering with the
anthropic wire proven, so the wire itself is fine and the turn is blocked by the endpoint. Arm (ii), native-shape
tools with no `mcp__` name, is UNMEASURED. Arm (iii), a whitelisted `mcp__*` subset, is **UNRUN — and must not be
treated as answered**: the recorded 403 was produced by a turn carrying the WHOLE tool array, and a body enumerating
every `mcp__` tool in an all-tools request says nothing about a subset request. Record all bodies in the row note.
**A second correction to the same evidence:** the raw 403 block transcribed in that narrative names the plugin figma
tools and our own session tools and is self-described as truncated; the dp-gitlab name usually quoted for it comes
from a SUMMARY of the row, not from the record — and it is the one name in that list that could bear on arm (iii) at
all, because that server IS one of the reference's eight whitelisted names. Read the record, not the summary.
**Step 2 — implement the branch the result selects.** **Branch C is NOT selectable as written:** it defers to DA2.2
and DA2.3, which the owner sent to their own plan on 2026-09-05; if the subset passes, the honest outcome is "no wire
change beyond what DA2.2 and DA2.3 build", stated as such.
**WHERE the branch may land is decided by the page, not by convenience.** Branch A written at the wire contradicts
`llm-gateway-protocol.md#feature-pass-through` and `llm-gateway-protocol.md#forward-as-open-lists` on the PASSTHROUGH
arm — the arm this probe is about — while branch B (tools omitted for a restricted anthropic-wire target, with the
DA2.6 line naming what is unavailable) contradicts neither and is the smaller change. Weigh that before choosing.
**The seam a branch extends already exists** and does not need minting: `classifyModelTarget` answers restricted, and
`noteOutboundTools` already fires on all three tool-carrying arms INCLUDING the untranslated passthrough.
**Live-checklist state:** the case `G3-WIRE` in `docs/testing/cdp-live-checklist.md` still reads a 2026-08-21 failure
and was NOT re-driven on the 1.37.0 rig day. Done means the recorded bodies, one branch implemented, and a fresh
verdict on that row.
**Native mechanism:** none — no runtime offers a target-conditioned tools-off mode; `P3.363` measured all three
deferral mechanisms and found none target-conditioned.
**Commit:** `feat(mcp): anthropic-on-corp-proxy turns work under the measured tools mode`

### DA2.5 (W5): loopback MCP server auth (№6) — REFUTED as a defect; two sentences remain

**THE DEFECT DOES NOT EXIST IN THIS TREE, in both halves, and one more claim about what is missing is refuted too.**
1. `packages/shared/src/mcp/pool-server.ts` symbol `McpPoolServer` carries a per-instance 32-byte random bearer,
   compared in symbol `isAuthorized` with a length pre-check and a constant-time compare, and an unauthenticated
   request is answered 401 BEFORE any route work; the server binds loopback only. That landed in `b995cc93a`
   (2026-07-25) — FOUR WEEKS BEFORE this plan was drafted — so the row's "no token" premise was already false when it
   was written.
2. `LocalMcpHttpServer` is NOT a symbol in this repository and never was: an absence probe over `packages apps
   scripts` answers **files=0** (positive control, `McpPoolServer`, answered 17 on 2026-09-12), and the only two hits
   tree-wide are this plan and its research spec, both documents. It is nessy-cli's vendored desktop copy, where the
   gap is real and unfixed at their HEAD. **The research base attributed their gap to "our copies" on shared-lineage
   grounds, and that inference cost this plan a Phase-2 row.**
3. **The negative tripwire an earlier reading called "the whole build" ALREADY EXISTS, and it landed in the same
   commit as the bearer.** `packages/shared/src/mcp/__tests__/proxy-tool-names.test.ts` case "rejects missing and
   wrong bearer credentials before MCP protocol handling" loops over an absent and a wrong bearer, asserts 401 on
   each, and then asserts that no session was created — pinning that the refusal precedes protocol handling, which is
   the stronger property. A search that only looked at the suites reading the bearer POSITIVELY missed it; the file
   that carries it has a name that does not suggest auth.
**The audit's question — "all three consumers" — answers itself.** Only `codex-runtime` starts an HTTP pool server
(`packages/shared/src/agent/backend/factory.ts` symbol `BACKEND_CAPABILITIES`), and the token reaches it by NAME and
never by value: the config writer emits a bearer-token ENV VAR name into the server block and excludes it from the
child's own env dump.

**WHAT SURVIVES — the whole row, size XS, DOC-ONLY, no production change:**
- **(a)** There is no `Origin` or `Host` check on any loopback listener (an absence probe over the pool server answers
  0 lines; control, the loopback address, answers 4). Not exploitable behind a required 32-byte bearer with no CORS
  headers — but the Streamable-HTTP guidance asks for one, so the absence belongs in a comment as a DECISION rather
  than sitting there as an oversight.
- **(b)** Two OTHER loopback listeners are not what this row named and each owes one sentence:
  `packages/shared/src/agent/backend/codex/session-callback-bridge.ts` authenticates by session-id-in-body (a 403 on
  mismatch, checked before a port is even resolved) and carries no bearer; and
  `packages/codex-responses-adapter/src/server.ts` has NO inbound authorization at all (probe: 0 lines; control, its
  JSON reply helper, 22) while holding the upstream credential.
- **(c)** The row's third Files-line clause, an empty headers list, was never tested in either direction. Probe it or
  strike it; leaving it is a third of the row's own premise unexamined.
**If the finding is ever to be acted on for `LocalMcpHttpServer`, it is a REPORT TO THAT PRODUCT, not a change here.**
**Native mechanism:** none — loopback MCP auth is entirely ours; no runtime supplies it.
**Commit:** `docs(mcp): the loopback pool bearer, the Origin decision, and two listeners this row did not name`

### DA2.6 (W6): the external-model restriction is VISIBLE, without a log (ODR-2 copy) — 🟨 PARTIALLY LANDED

**HALF LANDED, ON THE WRONG CHANNEL.** `P3.363` (`cb02270d0`, `e8f14543e`, `731bfa630`, `89f28509e`, sink
`d93109114`) built and wired `noteModelTargetToolEgress` on all three runtimes —
`packages/shared/src/agent/backend/factory.ts` at backend construction (target only, because the session's MCP tool
set does not exist yet there), and `claude-agent.ts` and `codex-agent.ts` through `onOutboundTools`, both carrying the
actual tool names. **But its default emit is a console warning, which a packaged Electron build never writes**, and
none of the three call sites injects a different sink — so the user sees nothing. That is the gap this row closes.
**i18n is absent:** `externalModel` and `whitelist` score zero keys in the English locale, and the only restricted-*
keys there belong to the managed model allowlist, a different mechanism. **No renderer reads the classifier:** the
four files under `apps/` that mention the module are two API-setup modules and their tests, all docblock citations.
**ONE HALF SUPERSEDED — DROP IT.** The in-session notice "corp-proxy external model: N MCP servers hidden" describes a
state that does not exist and is not scheduled: nothing is withheld today, the classifier documents its verdict as
advisory, and the owner sent the filter to its own plan. **What survives is the DISCLOSURE**, which the module's own
docblock argues is right under EITHER resolution of the DP-5 collision — telling a user their MCP tool schemas are
leaving for an external vendor.
**Scope, therefore:** a badge on MCP and Sources rows for a restricted-classified target; a once-per-session line
naming that this model's target receives the session's MCP tool schemas; a line on the model picker for such models;
en and ru keys with the i18n gates green. **Subscription and BYO connections show NO restriction UI (DP-5)** — and
that clause must be RE-READ against DA2.1's resolution before the badge is placed, because today's predicate would
badge a BYO endpoint at a vendor.
**READ THE COPY, DO NOT DRAFT IT (ODR-2, §READ-do-not-INVENT tier 2):** nessy-cli renders exactly this badge at HEAD —
`packages/cli/src/ui/components/mcp/steps/ServerListStep.tsx` draws a lock glyph plus "for external models" on a
server its all-servers accessor returned in the blocked set, fed by its config's allow-check. The placement and the
wording are read rather than invented; the owner reviews once at close-out.
**IN-ZONE XS FIX, in the same commit:** `packages/shared/src/agent/codex-agent.ts`'s `onOutboundTools` docblock still says the call "is correct and SUPPRESSED" until the dedup key gains a tools tier — and a SECOND docblock in the module that owns the key is incomplete for the same reason (WA-0 2026-09-13): `packages/shared/src/agent/backend/internal/drivers/upstream-target.ts` symbol `emittedModelTargetNotices` still says "One notice per distinct (class, host, namespace) per process" while the key carries a FOURTH term (` +tools`) and the function's own inline comment says the opposite. Fix both in the same commit — and `89f28509e`, one of this row's
own landed commits, ADDED that tier the same day, with the parity row recording "the sink is no longer suppressed". A
comment asserting a live limitation of a DIFFERENT module, falsified and unguarded: the exact class AGENTS.md records.
A reader of that file today would conclude the codex disclosure is dead when it is not.
**Native mechanism:** none — no runtime discloses its own tool egress, so this is host-side and uniform by
construction, which is the second half of the native-first rule: make the residual difference honest.
**Commit:** `feat(ui): external-model MCP restriction is visible`

## Phase 3 — MCP pool hardening

### DA3.1 (H1): stdio teardown leaves grandchildren behind on the UNSUPERVISED path (№7, RE-SCOPED)

**THE ROW'S PREMISE IS REFUTED WHERE IT POINTS, AND THE DEFECT SURVIVES WHERE IT DOES NOT. Build only where the leak
is.**
**The path the row names needs NOTHING.** `packages/server-core/src/pi-harness/process-supervisor.ts` has exactly ONE
spawn site and it carries `detached` off win32; its `signalTree` issues a negative-pid kill on POSIX and a tree
taskkill on Windows; `stop()` calls it with SIGTERM then SIGKILL. A real grandchild proof exists in its own suite: a
subshell that would write a marker after the parent is signalled never writes it. **Both mechanisms predate this plan
(`feba3c424` 2026-07-22 introduced the detach, `b0053399e` 2026-07-19 the tree signal), so the research base's
sentence — "our `ProcessSupervisorPort` covers the direct child; it does not cover `npx`/`uvx`/`pnpm dlx`
grandchildren" — was WRONG WHEN WRITTEN, not stale.** The plugin-MCP npx case it cited is the COVERED half, and the
mechanism that makes it covered is worth naming so the split is not assumed:
`packages/shared/src/mcp/runtime-pool-servers.ts` drops a plugin-bearing stdio server outright when its supervised
runtime authority is incomplete, and otherwise rewrites the command through the plugin executable resolver — a
fail-CLOSED guard. Without it an incomplete authority would fall THROUGH to the unsupervised branch and the whole
covered/uncovered split would not hold. (`packages/shared/src/mcp/client.ts` also throws when a plugin stdio config
arrives with no supervisor, which is the weaker half of the same proof.)

**WHAT LEAKS is the branch the row never names.** A stdio MCP config WITHOUT a plugin runtime takes the else-branch in
`client.ts` and gets the vendored SDK's `StdioClientTransport`, which spawns with NO detach and closes by signalling
the DIRECT CHILD (SIGTERM then SIGKILL). Nothing above it adds a teardown: `NessyMcpClient.close()` sets a flag and
awaits the SDK client's close — no kill, no process group. That branch serves:
- every native, project and user stdio entry;
- all **28** builtin dp servers (`packages/shared/src/mcp/builtin-dp-servers.ts` emits stdio and carries no plugin
  runtime);
- `packages/shared/src/mcp/validation.ts` symbol `validateStdioMcpConnection`, a THIRD unsupervised spawn site nobody
  had named, which builds its own transport to probe a user-entered server and whose own comment names `uv tool run`,
  `npx` and `pipx` cold installs. It is the shortest-lived process in the tree and the most likely to be a wrapper.

**SCOPE CORRECTION — do not carry one framing across all three sites.** The dp set is NOT npx-shaped: its materializer
emits the RESOLVED ABSOLUTE dp path with an MCP subcommand, and its docblock says why (an agent subprocess does not
necessarily inherit the PATH that found it). For the dp set the residue is dp's OWN forked children, which is a
different and UNMEASURED claim. The genuinely wrapper-shaped populations are the user-written entries and the
validator.
**Uniform across all three runtimes, and provable at the emitting site:** the host pool owns these processes, and
codex reaches them through the loopback pool rather than spawning its own — the codex config writer emits exactly TWO
server entries, `session` and `sources`, and states that every operator-configured MCP server reaches codex aggregated
behind one loopback endpoint.
**NATIVE MECHANISM — the rule is enable before building, and the host already has TWO.** The supervisor's group kill,
and `packages/shared/src/mcp/headers-helper.ts`, which spawns its helper in its OWN PROCESS GROUP so a deadline can
signal the whole tree — the only other detached spawn in that package. **So the cheapest honest fix is to give the
unsupervised stdio branch the same detached-group treatment (a thin transport beside the supervised one), NOT to port
the reference's process-table sweep.** Their sweep exists because their transports are NOT detached; porting it here
would be a second mechanism for a solved problem. Keep the sweep only as the NAMED RESIDUE for a descendant that
calls `setsid()` and leaves the group, and say so rather than building it. What IS transferable from the reference is
its fallback discipline: degrade gracefully and return empty so the OS reaps orphans.
**Files:** `packages/shared/src/mcp/client.ts` (the non-plugin transport branch), `packages/shared/src/mcp/validation.ts`
(`validateStdioMcpConnection`); tests beside each.
**Validation:** on a PACKAGED build — `electron:dev` runs built bundles, and the Electron file-descriptor floor makes a
per-teardown fork a real cost, so bound any fan-out.
**ADJACENT MEASUREMENT, and a trap inside it:** coverage `C3.103` owes a three-runtime process-tree-termination
reading with the signalled PID named, and its done-means says any runtime that leaks a process tree gets a row of its
own. That rig is the cheapest instrument for this row's packaged-build clause — **but it drives BASH children, which
are supervised and group-killed, so it will come back GREEN while this leak is untouched.** A test whose two arms
cannot disagree is the default-flip trap this repo has already paid for; add the MCP arm DELIBERATELY.
**Size:** M (was S) — three call sites, a Windows arm, and the packaged-build validation.
**Commit:** `fix(mcp): unsupervised stdio teardown sweeps wrapper-spawned descendants`

### DA3.2 (H2): the sharing lever is transport-blind (№8, residue of P3.371) — 🟨 PARTIALLY LANDED

**THE MEASUREMENT AND THE MECHANISM LANDED as parity `P3.371` (`09d3e94af`, 2026-09-04), and the DEFAULT was decided
the other way — deliberately, transparently, and with the reason written down.** This plan's tracker read as though
the measurement, the keying, the descriptor-store split and the operator lever were all still to be built: three
quarters of a size-M row that is already done. Anyone dispatching Phase 3 from the old tracker alone would have
rebuilt it.
**At HEAD, in `packages/shared/src/mcp/mcp-pool.ts`:** `mcpConnectionKey(slug, config, isolationTerm?)` appends the
leaser id as a FOURTH key element when the pool runs per-leaser; `toolDescriptorStoreKey` STRIPS it so the durable
descriptor cache stays keyed by the server — the unbounded-store trap this row's research never saw, made sharper by
the fact that the descriptor store's prune has no production caller; `McpConnectionIsolation` is shared or per-leaser,
selected from an env lever through `mcpConnectionIsolationFromEnv()` with a constructor report on a typo; and
`reportSharedDynamicHeaderConnections` NAMES, once per connection, every shared connection whose credentials the
digest cannot see. The docblock states the posture in this repo's transparent-and-simple voice: shared is the default
because it is what shipped and because the alternative's connection-count cost is paid per session per server, and
the alternative is a lever rather than a rewrite. The measurement this row demanded is case (a) of
`packages/shared/src/mcp/__tests__/mcp-pool-connection-isolation.test.ts`: three sessions, one connection shared and
three isolated.
**THE RESIDUE IS EXACT AND UNBUILT: the lever is TRANSPORT-BLIND.** `isolationTermFor` is one line and returns the
leaser id whenever the pool is per-leaser, with NO read of the transport type, even though the config is in scope at
the call site; the poolable-transport concept scores zero files here (positive control, needle `connectionIsolation`: 13 lines over 2 files — name the needle, because the TYPE name `McpConnectionIsolation` answers 6 lines over 1 file for the same fact; WA-0 2026-09-13). So an operator who wants the HTTP and SSE stream-state protection this row was written
for must ALSO pay one stdio PROCESS per session per server — the expensive half the row explicitly wanted to keep
shared.
**Behavior:** make isolation a per-TRANSPORT decision rather than a pool-wide one, with a default table stated where
an operator reads it: `http` and `sse` isolate per leaser (some MCP HTTP servers bind auth context or conversation
memory to the stream, and our sharing radius — every session plus browse in a workspace — is wider than the reference
implementations'); `stdio` and `ws` stay shared (one process per session per server is the cost `P3.371` refused).
The env lever keeps both existing spellings and gains the ability to force either default, so nothing an operator has
set today changes meaning.
**Files:** `packages/shared/src/mcp/mcp-pool.ts` (`isolationTermFor`, the isolation type and its env reader); tests in
the existing isolation suite.
**Do NOT re-do the keying.** The four-element form and the store-key stripper exist; `activeLeasersForConnection` and
the elicitation attribution the research named as the contract change are still present and were NOT simplified,
because the default stayed shared.
**Native mechanism:** none — neither the Claude nor the codex pages state a sharing radius, which is why `P3.371`
decided it in the open; this row extends that same decision by one axis rather than inventing a posture. The
reference is exactly this shape (`mcp-pool-key.ts` symbols `POOLED_TRANSPORTS_DEFAULT` and `isPoolable`, with HTTP and
SSE excluded by default and an operator opt-in), and it is the piece `P3.371` did not take.
**Size:** S.
**Commit:** `fix(mcp): the connection-isolation lever becomes a per-transport decision`

### DA3.3 (H3): pin the whole-config digest against a future canonical-fields refactor (№9, RE-SPECIFIED) — 🟨

**THE INVARIANT HOLDS BY CONSTRUCTION, one of its rows is already pinned, and TWO of the row's clauses are premise
errors — a table written to its literal wording would ASSERT A FALSEHOOD.**
1. **By construction:** `mcpConnectionKey` digests a sha256 over the WHOLE server config through a stable-JSON
   normaliser (undefined values filtered, keys sorted, recursive), so any credential-bearing field PRESENT in the
   config necessarily splits the key. The projection-shaped bug the reference fixed cannot exist here.
2. **Already pinned:** case (c) of `packages/shared/src/mcp/__tests__/mcp-pool-connection-isolation.test.ts` —
   "a static credential DOES split the key" — drives two sessions differing only in an authorization header and
   asserts two connects and two different keys. It landed inside `P3.371` (`09d3e94af`).
3. **PREMISE ERROR ONE:** the four OAuth field names this row enumerates do not exist in our config union.
   `packages/shared/src/agent/backend/types.ts` interface `McpOAuthConfig` carries exactly a metadata URL and scopes,
   with a docblock saying "Neither is ever a secret, so both are safe on a config object that gets logged and hashed".
   Those four names are the reference's, not ours. (Positive control, which is what makes the absence falsifiable: the
   client-secret needle IS greppable across ten other files in this tree, so the zero over our MCP surface is real.)
4. **PREMISE ERROR TWO, and it is the one that matters:** "configs differing ONLY in credential fields MUST produce
   different pool keys" has a MEASURED, DELIBERATE EXCEPTION at HEAD. A dynamic headers helper is digested as its SPEC
   and never as what it RETURNS, so two sessions declaring the same helper share ONE connection carrying whichever
   token resolved first — documented in `mcpConnectionKey`'s own docblock, diagnosed once per connection by
   `reportSharedDynamicHeaderConnections`, and pinned by two further cases of the same suite.
**Behavior:** extend the EXISTING isolation suite rather than opening a second file. A table over the credential-bearing
fields that ACTUALLY exist in the config union — http/sse `headers` (case (c), keep it as the table's first row),
stdio `env`, stdio `envVars`, http/sse `bearerTokenEnvVar` — each asserting that two configs differing ONLY in that
field produce different keys. No test at HEAD covers the last three. Beside it, ONE explicit negative recording the
documented exception: two configs differing only in what a headers helper RESOLVES to are the same key, on purpose,
and the diagnostic is what carries that fact to the operator. Put `oauth` in as a NON-secret control, not as a
credential row — the field's own docblock says why.
**ONE MECHANICAL TRAP TO AVOID:** the key now takes an optional isolation term appended as a fourth element, which the
store-key helper strips. **Build both sides of every inequality through the SAME isolation posture**, or the table
compares a three-element key with a four-element one and passes for the wrong reason.
**No production change. Size XS.**
**Native mechanism:** none — this is our own keying. The guard exists because the REFACTOR it protects against —
replacing the whole-config digest with a hand-written projection — is exactly how the reference acquired the bug: its
own canonicalising helper records the pre-fix leak by name, five fields that collapsed to one fingerprint and shared a
pool entry.
**Sequence after `I2.2`** if that row lands entry-level header plumbing, or re-run the table then.
**Commit:** `test(mcp): pool keys must differ on credential-only config changes`

### DA3.4 (NEW, nessy-cli delta): audit `nessy-server` against the donor daemon's fail-closed checklist

**Born 2026-09-12. AUDIT-FIRST; this row records verdicts, it does not authorise a build. Size M.**
**(a) The checklist.** The donor's daemon auth document is a ready-made audit list for `packages/server` and
`packages/server-core`, and every item is a yes-or-no verdict this repo can record: refuse to BOOT on a non-loopback
bind without a bearer token; refuse a require-auth flag without one; refuse a wildcard allowed-origin without one;
constant-time bearer comparison with hand-rolled scheme parsing rather than a regex; a port-keyed loopback Host
allowlist against DNS rebinding; deny browser origins by default; a health route exempt on loopback ONLY; and a
per-route mutation gate returning a DISTINCT "token required" code so a client can render a real hint instead of a
generic 401 — the same make-the-refusal-legible principle as DA1.12's distinct expiry code and DA2.5's Origin
decision. **Record a verdict PER ITEM. This audit did NOT open that path and states NO parity verdict** — naming an
absence without a probe is precisely the claim this repo's rules forbid, and an absence probe needs a positive control
on the same rig.
**Read their own self-declared weakness before copying anything:** their page records that a client identifier is
format-validated with no proof-of-possession, so a caller who observes one on the event stream can re-register it and
impersonate that originator in later permission votes. Do not import that shape.
**(b) The device-flow probe, and an earlier NULL here is REFUTED by donor SOURCE.** An earlier reading of this item
said "we have not checked whether the Nestor side supports a device flow for dp at all" and recommended probing. The
answer is one read away in the source, which §READ-do-not-INVENT puts ahead of a probe: their OAuth module declares a
DP-Auth DEVICE-CODE endpoint, a token endpoint, a client id, a scope and the RFC-8628 grant type, and their
device-flow provider is built on them. **So an RFC-8628 device flow for DP Auth EXISTS.** Ours is absent — every
device-code hit in this tree is a codex or copilot surface — and `packages/shared/src/auth/dp/session-broker.ts`
symbol `startRun` raises the interactive-login-unavailable error when non-interactive, so the env token is our only
headless route today.
**This row RECORDS that finding and nothing more.** A headless dp sign-in is a genuinely open native-first candidate,
but it is a PRODUCT POSTURE and needs an owner answer. Note the tension and the resolution honestly: **Target scheme
clause 2 declines an auto-opened browser, and a device flow shows a CODE rather than opening one, so the two do not
conflict — but that is an argument for asking, not a licence to build.**
**Native mechanism:** for (b), the runtime's own mechanism EXISTS and is the reason this is a candidate at all; for
(a) the daemon is ours and the checklist is evidence rather than authority (a different product, a different threat
model).
**Commit:** `docs(server): the headless daemon's auth posture, item by item, with its nulls recorded`

### DA3.5 (NEW, nessy-cli delta): an in-source divergence marker, `// [nessy-fork] <name>`

**Born 2026-09-12. Size S. OPTIONAL — it buys an index, not a behaviour; decline it in writing if the owner prefers no
new convention.**
**Behavior:** adopt a single-line in-source marker above each intentional departure from the donor — the BRACKETED form is the grep needle: `git grep -aF -- '[nessy-fork]' HEAD -- packages apps` answers 0 at HEAD while the bare token `nessy-fork` answers 5 lines, every one a temp-directory prefix in a fork-subagent test, so a bare-token convention would be born colliding (WA-0 2026-09-13), starting with the
dp-auth module, where two divergences are already written out in prose and would gain a grep-able name: the
exchange-versus-decode choice in `packages/shared/src/auth/dp/env-token.ts`, and the sole-writer posture of
`packages/shared/src/auth/dp/creds-store.ts`.
**Why it is worth taking, stated as a property rather than as tidiness:** a MARKER can only fail to RESOLVE; it cannot
go quietly false the way an asserted premise can. That is the failure AGENTS.md records at P6.551 — a comment
asserting what a DIFFERENT module does not do has no guard, and it cost a size-M plan row when the other module
started doing it. A named marker is the instrument this repo keeps reaching for when it says "grep the CLAIM, and
grep it SHORT".
**SKIP the donor's per-patch file and rollback-recipe ceremony** — our generated deviation register already covers
that half, and the registry is not even a complete index of their own changes (the one item with a direct defect twin
in our tree has no entry in it, which is why a future delta read must diff the SOURCE paths and not the registry).
**Native mechanism:** n/a — repo convention.
**Commit:** `chore(auth): name every deliberate divergence from nessy-cli in one grep-able form`

## Phase 4 — Close-out

### DA4.1 (C1): full regression, version bump, release notes, docs-site, cdp rows

Per the standing close-out rule (AGENTS.md §Editing Rules):
1. **Bump root AND `apps/electron` `package.json` in LOCKSTEP** to the next version current at execution time — do
   NOT pin a number now, and do NOT touch `bun.lock`: its electron workspace entry lags by a release or more and the
   frozen-lockfile install tolerates it, so never run an install in a shared tree mid-wave to "fix" it. **The old
   instruction "parity's own close-out lands first" is SPENT:** parity closed at 1.36.0, the coverage plan shipped
   1.37.0 (which is what both files read at HEAD, with release notes complete to it and no gap), and the owner's
   recorded sequencing puts wave 19, the runtime lifts, the coverage plan's own 1.38.0 close-out and a PAUSE ahead of
   this plan.
2. A NEW `apps/electron/resources/release-notes/<version>.md` — append-only history, title, date line, and the
   new/improved/fixed sections.
3. **docs-site pages, scoped to whichever rows actually land:** the credentials troubleshooting work from DA1.4,
   DA1.8, DA1.18 and DA1.19 (all four edit the same page — land them together or in a stated order), and an
   external-models MCP page from DA2.2 and DA2.6 carrying Phase 2's honest scope statement VERBATIM.
4. **`docs/testing/cdp-live-checklist.md`** gains one row per case in the single case-by-runs table, with narratives
   under `docs/testing/cdp-runs/`: gateway 401 self-heal with the managed-dp fallback, external-model tool filtering
   per runtime (dark theme for screenshots), and a loopback bearer probe. **None of the three exists yet** — an
   absence probe for their names over that file returns nothing against a control of 152 table rows — and DA2.4 also
   owes a fresh verdict on the existing `G3-WIRE` row.
5. **Regenerate BOTH derived files from a QUIET tree:** the deviation register, and the parity report — run the
   parity report's `--check` first, because it reads the FULL TEXT of every plan as a directory glob, so an untracked
   plan in the working tree lands in the output.

**GATE COMMAND AT HEAD, not as written in August.** The row's original list is now a SUBSET of the tree's own gates:
`typecheck:all` and `lint` are `run-gates.ts` chains, `validate:ci` is another, and the lint chain carries twenty-plus
steps that did not exist in August (NUL bytes, settings line citations, comment globs, conditional expect, matcher
mutation, minified citations, exports map, ledger citations, module-graph hooks, mock-module spread, i18n
reachability, live surface, …). Run `rm -rf release`, then `bun run validate:ci`, `bun run typecheck:all`,
`bun run test:shared:all` and `bun test`. **Read the FILE COUNT of every `bun test` run and prefix every path with a
leading `./`** — a bare path is a FILTER, not a path, and beside one matching filter the run goes green having
silently dropped the rest.
**SCOPE depends on the Ownership answer.** Under option A this close-out owns everything; under option B it shrinks to
DA0.1, DA1.9, Phase 2, Phase 3 and the new delta rows. **Check the twins' glyphs before sizing:** all seventeen `A3.*`
rows read open with an empty commit cell at HEAD, so nothing has moved upstream and the shrinkage is currently ZERO.
And remember `A3.15` is this plan's own DA0.1 by another name — whichever file runs it, it runs once.
**Size:** M for the mechanics; the docs-site half is sized by what lands.
**Commit:** the close-out's own message, one commit per step where the steps touch different files.

## What this refresh did NOT verify

**Recorded as nulls rather than left as silent gaps. Each one is a claim a lane must not inherit as settled.**

1. **The whitelist verifier workflow is not a repo artefact.** DA0.1's second half tells T0 to fold "the whitelist
   verifier's verdict (workflow `wf_0c437110-6dd`)" into the Phase 2 specs. That identifier occurs in exactly ONE FILE of this tree (three lines, all restatements of this claim — count files, not lines: WA-0 2026-09-13) — this plan's own — so no lane can read it. Its substance was folded into the W-rows in 2026-08-22 and
   is re-folded here; treat the workflow reference as closed with a null, not as a pending read.
2. **DA2.4's probe series needs the owner's entitled credential and was not run.** Arm (ii) (native-shape tools with
   no `mcp__` name) is UNMEASURED and arm (iii) (a whitelisted subset) is UNRUN. Nothing in this refresh moved either.
   A fake-key BYO card cannot stand in.
3. **No parity verdict is stated for `nessy-server`'s bind, bearer and origin posture** (DA3.4 half (a)). This audit
   did not open `packages/server`'s auth path, and naming an absence there without a probe and a positive control is
   the claim this repo's rules forbid.
4. **The gateway's live behaviour on a flat reasoning key is unmeasured** (DA1.16). The adapter half is answered from the source. CLOSED by WA-e 2026-09-13 with live turns on the default coder model at temperature 0: NO effort key → `reasoning_tokens: 0` in three runs (the 'silently defaults to high' claim is REFUTED — no turn is billed at high invisibly), and flat `reasoning_effort` vs nested `reasoning.effort` returned byte-identical usage (the 'nested form is ignored' claim is REFUTED). C4.37's server half is answered by the same reading; `supportsThinking` already defaults true.
5. **`exchangeDpToken` has never been driven with a real Spirit-IAM delegated token** (DA1.15). The risk is a hypothesis about the exchange endpoint, not a reproduction. STAYS OPEN after WA-a's measurement (2026-09-13, fabricated values, six arms + controls): the gateway answers 401 with a byte-identical 207-byte `EUNAUTHORIZED` body to a JWT-shaped bearer, an opaque bearer, a `Nestor-Token` and NO credential at all, on both `/api/v2/token` and `/api/v1/cli/models` — so a refusal of a real delegated token would be indistinguishable from every other refusal; only a real token settles it (the owner's).
6. **No 403 has been MEASURED on the gateway chat endpoint** (DA1.14). The model fetcher records a 401 for a wrong
   header and a 5xx for an unparseable credential; the 403 arm closes a classifier asymmetry with the donor, not a
   reproduced failure.
7. **Two DA3.1 claims are unmeasured in opposite directions.** Whether dp's own forked children survive a teardown of
   the dp MCP servers is unmeasured (the wrapper framing does not transfer to that population); and the coverage rig
   that would validate the packaged-build clause drives BASH children, which are group-killed, so it cannot see this
   leak without a deliberately added MCP arm.
8. **DA2.5's third Files-line clause (an empty headers list) was not tested in either direction**, and the raw 403
   body behind DA2.4 was read only in its TRUNCATED transcription — the untruncated body has never been read, which is
   why the dp-gitlab name in it cannot be confirmed.
9. **P3.334's codex ruling is carried at a superseded tag.** It was read at `rust-v0.148.0`. Re-read at the shipped tag `rust-v0.154.0` by WA-0 on 2026-09-13: **the ruling STANDS in all three clauses.** `codex-rs/codex-api/src/api_bridge.rs` symbol `map_api_error` still classifies by status and still maps a `misalignment_policy_violation` body to a policy violation (on 400 OR 403, as it already did at 148 — P3.334 quoted only the 403 half); 401 is still intercepted before the mapper, but the anchor MOVED — the guard is now `codex-rs/model-provider/src/provider.rs` symbol `is_recoverable_auth_error`, matching `StatusCode::UNAUTHORIZED` and nothing else, feeding `client.rs`'s `handle_unauthorized`; and `codex-rs/codex-client/src/retry.rs` symbol `RetryOn::should_retry` is byte-identical between the two tags, retrying only 429 and 5xx. This null CLOSES.
10. **Every count in this file is a reading with a date, taken while the tree moved.** The audit's readers pinned one
    commit, its skeptics three others, and this file's own spot-checks a fifth; lanes committed throughout. Re-run the
    probe rather than quote a number — including the 28 builtin dp servers, the 12 prefix sites, the 71 injected jwt

## Hand-back to the owner and to AGENTS.md (NOT applied by this refresh)

**This plan does not edit `AGENTS.md`. One correction is owed there and is recorded here so it is not lost.**
§Gateway Facts says the JWT prefill file is "SHARED with nessy-cli, its shape is nessy-cli's contract". **At nessy-cli
HEAD that is only half true: NOTHING in their `packages/*/src` writes `dp_auth_creds.json` any more** — their auth
service persists to the shared oauth file instead, and their save helpers have no caller outside their own module and
a stale build directory — **while FOUR of their modules READ it** (their statist client, their DLH client, their
models client and their error client). **The SHAPE is still theirs; the FILE is ours to keep alive, and we are the
only writer in the ecosystem.** Our own seed reader's docblock already records the premise correctly. The practical
consequence is on DA1.8: a stale or absent file is now a CLI-side interactive-login trigger on the user's machine, not
only our own needs-login.
